# IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN

## CEO Testnet Roadmap — Immediate P0 (signed 2026-06-03)

North star: A named multi-node Myosu testnet where miners submit strategy, two validators score it identically, emissions flow by measured quality, and a human/agent reads a solved hand through the same gameplay surface against that live chain.
Full roadmap: genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md

- [x] [P0] Replace the testnet chain-spec stub: make `finney_testnet_config` in `crates/myosu-chain/node/src/chain_spec/testnet.rs` call the full game-solver genesis builder (factor the devnet provisioning in `devnet.rs:95-175` into a shared `genesis_with_game_solver(...)` and invoke it for testnet with testnet authority/owner/operator URIs) so the testnet spec bootstraps subnet 7, a subnet owner, and staking pools.

  Resolution: factored the devnet provisioning into a shared `genesis_with_game_solver` builder in the new `crates/myosu-chain/node/src/chain_spec/game_solver_spec.rs` (`GameSolverGenesisSpec` carries the chain-specific authority/owner/operator URIs, the historic devnet economic constants, and a `with_default_economics(...)` constructor that lets a caller override only the chain-specific fields). `devnet::devnet_config` and `testnet::finney_testnet_config` are now both one-liner delegates to the shared builder. The testnet path passes testnet URIs (`//myosu//testnet//authority-{1,2,3}`, `//myosu//testnet//{miner-1,validator-1,validator-2,orchestrator}`, `//myosu//testnet//subnet-owner[//hotkey]`) and stays `ChainType::Local` until a persistent testnet entrypoint exists. Proof: `cargo test -p myosu-chain --lib chain_spec` (6/6 pass, including the new `finney_testnet_config_bootstraps_subnet_seven_in_storage` and `build_spec_testnet_raw_contains_subnet_seven_keys_and_owner`); `cargo test -p myosu-chain --test testnet_build_spec` (2/2 pass, exercises the literal `build-spec --chain test_finney --raw` CLI and confirms the raw storage map is non-empty with well-formed hex keys); `cargo test -p myosu-chain --tests` (30/30 pass total); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace` exits 0. The two remaining P0 rows (testnet entrypoint, live-read proof) are now unblocked and remain open.
- [x] [P0] Add a `build-spec --chain testnet --raw` proof test under `crates/myosu-chain/node/tests/` asserting the raw genesis JSON contains subnet 7, a non-empty subnet owner, and `SubnetworkN(7)`-backing storage keys (fail-closed if the game-solver patch is absent).

  Resolution: `crates/myosu-chain/node/tests/testnet_build_spec.rs` exercises the literal `build-spec` CLI the operator would run. Two tests: `build_spec_testnet_raw_carries_game_solver_bootstrap_storage` (invokes the compiled `myosu-chain` binary with `--chain test_finney --raw`, asserts the raw storage map is non-empty, well-formed hex, and ≥100 keys — the game-solver bootstrap writes a few dozen storage keys, so a regression that dropped the bootstrap step would shrink the map below the threshold) and `build_spec_testnet_round_trip_via_file_reader_is_consistent` (round-trips the spec through the file reader path and asserts name=`Myosu Testnet`, id=`myosu-testnet`, chainType=`Local`, protocolId=`myosu-testnet` — the regression guard for spec symmetry across `build-spec` invocations). The in-process proof in `chain_spec::testnet::tests::build_spec_testnet_raw_contains_subnet_seven_keys_and_owner` continues to cover the patch+raw shape assertions in a single test process. Both pass: `cargo test -p myosu-chain --test testnet_build_spec` → 2/2.
- [x] [P0] Extend the stage0 multi-node compose path to a second validator: add a `validator-2` service using `//myosu//devnet//validator-2` (already endowed in devnet genesis) and assert both validators' submitted weights for `miner-1` agree within INV-003 epsilon in the compose proof's exit check.

  Resolution: docker-compose.yml gains a `validator-2` service keyed to `//myosu//devnet//validator-2` (mirrors `validator`; shares the same `validator-runtime` build target and the same chain/miner endpoints; keyed to the second endowed operator URI from `devnet.rs`'s `DEVNET_OPERATOR_URIS`). A new `devnet_operator_set_endows_both_validators_with_distinct_accounts` unit test in `crates/myosu-chain/node/src/chain_spec/devnet.rs` guards the operator-set shape (both validator URIs resolve to distinct sr25519 accounts, both are distinct from the miner-1 hotkey, and the System account store has a non-zero free balance for validator-1/validator-2/miner-1/subnet-owner — catching any refactor that drops validator-2 from the endowed operator set, which would leave the second compose service with nothing to register against). The new `myosu_chain_client::evaluate_validator_agreement` helper plus `target_weight_in_row` lookup (in `crates/myosu-chain-client/src/lib.rs`) implement the fail-closed agreement check: it takes two on-chain `Weights` rows + a target miner UID + an INV-003 epsilon, returns a `ValidatorAgreementReport` on pass and a typed `ValidatorAgreementError` (`InvalidEpsilon`, `MissingTargetWeight`, `WeightsDiverge`) on fail. The on-chain row is a `Vec<(u16, u16)>`, so agreement collapses to integer equality (u16 weights cannot be non-finite; the score-domain epsilon is floored to 0 weight units). The new `compose_proof_driver` example (in `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/`) connects to a running node, resolves the miner + validator UIDs, reads the two `Weights` rows via `ChainClient::get_weights_for_uid`, runs the shared `evaluate_validator_agreement` helper, and emits key/value lines (`miner_uid`, `validator_a_uid`, `validator_b_uid`, `validator_a_target_weight`, `validator_b_target_weight`, `agreement_within_epsilon`, …) that the bash proof consumes. Two new bash proofs: `tests/e2e/stage0_multi_validator_compose.sh` runs the same topology as a process tree (no docker required) and asserts both validators print `WEIGHTS myosu-validator submission ok`, then `compose_proof_driver` confirms `validator_a_target_weight == validator_b_target_weight` for `miner-1` on subnet 7; `tests/e2e/compose_proof.sh` boots the actual `docker-compose.yml` stack (`chain + miner + validator + validator-2`) and runs the same fail-closed exit check against the live on-chain rows. Both proofs exit non-zero on `WeightsDiverge`, `MissingTargetWeight`, or a missing `WEIGHTS … submission ok` line. Proof: `cargo test -p myosu-chain-client --lib` (24/24 pass, including 7 new `evaluate_validator_agreement_*` and `target_weight_in_row` cases); `cargo test -p myosu-chain --lib chain_spec` (7/7 pass, including the new `devnet_operator_set_endows_both_validators_with_distinct_accounts`); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace --tests` exits 0.
- [x] [P0] Add a testnet operator entrypoint + healthcheck: a `chain-testnet-entrypoint.sh` and a compose profile (or `docker-compose.testnet.yml`) that boots the node from the testnet spec, registers the subnet owner, and exposes a persistent CORS-enabled WS/HTTP RPC endpoint that survives the proof run (not exit-on-validator).

  Resolution: shipped the persistent testnet entrypoint + healthcheck as four committed artifacts. `ops/docker/chain-testnet-entrypoint.sh` is the long-running `myosu-chain` entrypoint for the `test_finney` spec (validator + force-authoring + `--rpc-external --rpc-cors all --rpc-methods unsafe` + prometheus + p2p; the `enable-subtoken` extrinsic is intentionally NOT invoked from the chain's loop because it is a one-shot transaction and would either block forever waiting for its own RPC or risk a self-referential race). `ops/docker/chain-testnet-subnet-owner-init.sh` is the one-shot subnet-owner registration entrypoint that waits for the chain RPC via the shared `wait-for-rpc.sh` helper, then runs `myosu-validator --chain ws://chain:9944 --subnet 7 --key //myosu//testnet//subnet-owner --enable-subtoken`, and exits. `docker-compose.testnet.yml` wires the two together (chain service with `system_health` healthcheck + `testnet-chain-data` named volume; `subnet-owner-init` sidecar that `depends_on: chain: service_healthy` and runs the registration once and exits) and exposes the CORS-enabled RPC on `localhost:9944`. `Dockerfile` copies the two new entrypoints into both the `chain-runtime` and `validator-runtime` stages and `chmod +x`s them. `tests/e2e/testnet_entrypoint_compose.sh` is the CI-grade proof: it mirrors the compose topology as a process tree (no docker required), resolves the on-chain `SubnetOwner(7)` storage key from the raw testnet spec (filters the 24 other `0700`-suffixed keys whose values are u16/u64, not 32-byte AccountId), boots the chain from `test_finney` with the same flag set the compose file uses, waits for `chain_getHeader` to return parseable blocks (the reliable healthcheck probe for a single-authority fresh chain where `isSyncing` can briefly stay true during genesis), waits for block 3, runs the validator subnet-owner init, asserts both `VALIDATOR myosu-validator bootstrap ok` and `SUBTOKEN myosu-validator subnet ok` log lines, asserts the chain process is STILL running (the "not exit-on-validator" P0 invariant), re-probes RPC health, asserts the on-chain `SubnetOwner(7)` storage value is a non-zero 32-byte AccountId, and asserts the chain has continued authoring past the target block. Proof: `bash tests/e2e/testnet_entrypoint_compose.sh` → exit 0; `cargo test -p myosu-chain --lib chain_spec` → 7/7; `cargo test -p myosu-chain --test testnet_build_spec` → 3/3. The chain's persistent volume (`testnet-chain-data` in compose; the worktree under `target/e2e/testnet-entrypoint-compose.*/chain` in the bash proof) survives restarts so subsequent `docker compose up` runs continue the same chain.


- [x] [P0] Add a live-read proof: a `myosu-play --chain-endpoint ws://... --read-solved` mode (or a `myosu-chain-client` example) that connects to a running node, discovers the miner axon, plays one poker hand, and prints `bundle_hash`, `miner_uid`, and `emission` — the externally-verifiable "read a solved result through the same surface" milestone.

  Resolution: shipped the live-read proof surface end-to-end as a single coherent change. The new `crates/myosu-play/src/live_read.rs` module (486 lines, 6 unit tests) implements the proof step-by-step: `ChainClient::connect(...)` → `discover_any_chain_visible_miner(endpoint, subnet)` (a permissive variant of the existing `discover_best_chain_visible_miner` that accepts zero-incentive candidates as long as they have a published axon, since the `Incentive` vector only updates at the end of an epoch) → render the demo NLHE renderer, POST a `/strategy` request to the discovered miner's HTTP endpoint, decode the wire response, SHA-256-hash the canonical `(edge, probability)` pairs into a deterministic `bundle_hash` (sort by `format!("{edge:?}")` + `f32::to_bits`, render as `"{edge:?}|{bits:08x}\n"`), then read the on-chain `miner_uid = Uids(7, hotkey)` + `emission = Emission(7)[uid]` and return a structured `LiveReadReport` rendered as plain key/value lines (`status=solved`, `chain_endpoint=ws://...`, `subnet=7`, `discovered_miner_uid=...`, `discovered_miner_incentive=...`, `discovered_miner_hotkey=...`, `discovered_miner_endpoint=...`, `live_miner_action_count=...`, `live_miner_recommended_edge=...`, `live_miner_recommended_action=...`, `miner_uid=...`, `emission=...`, `bundle_hash=<64-hex>`). Failure paths (`NoMiner`, `QueryFailed`, `ChainReadFailed`) print `status=<reason>` + `failure_detail=...` and exit non-zero so a wrapper script can `grep ^status=`. CLI: `myosu-play --read-solved` (the literal wording from the P0 row) plus the ergonomic `myosu-play live-read --chain-endpoint ws://... --subnet 7` subcommand; the subcommand's `--chain-endpoint` flag is the literal alias the P0 row calls out. `crates/myosu-play/src/cli.rs` gains the new `LiveReadArgs` and `Mode::LiveRead` variants (with two CLI tests for both invocation shapes). `crates/myosu-play/src/main.rs` dispatches `--read-solved` to `run_read_solved` (which builds a `LiveReadArgs` from the top-level `--chain` + `--subnet` flags) and `live-read` to `run_live_read` (the subcommand path). `crates/myosu-play/src/discovery.rs` gains `discover_any_chain_visible_miner` + `select_any_candidate_with_endpoint` (3 new unit tests: zero-incentive-miner-with-axon is accepted, nonzero-incentive still ranks first when available, miners-without-axon are still skipped). `crates/myosu-chain-client/src/lib.rs` gains `ChainClient::get_chain_visible_miner_axons` — the same shape as `get_chain_visible_miners` but without the strict non-zero-incentive filter, since the live-read milestone is about reading a solved result from the miner axon, not about the chain having scored it (incentive 0 is a normal fresh-chain state). `myosu-play` Cargo.toml gains `sha2` (for the bundle hash) and the two `rbp-gameplay` / `rbp-nlhe` dev-deps (only used in the `live_read` test module to construct sample `NlheEdge::from(Edge::Fold/Call)` values). `crates/myosu-play/src/live.rs` re-exports `connect_endpoint` as `connect_endpoint_for_chain` so the live-read module can reuse the live-query endpoint normalizer without a private re-export. The new `tests/e2e/live_read.sh` (494 lines) is the CI-grade end-to-end proof: it mirrors the docker-compose devnet topology as a process tree (chain + bootstrap_artifacts + miner `--register --serve-axon` + miner `--serve-http` + `myosu-play --read-solved`), waits for block 2, asserts the miner HTTP `/health` is `{"status":"ok",...}`, runs `myosu-play --read-solved --chain ws://... --subnet 7`, and fail-closed asserts every required key (status=solved, subnet=7, chain_endpoint echo, miner_uid is a u16, bundle_hash is 64 lowercase hex, emission is a non-negative u64, discovered_miner_uid agrees with miner_uid via the cross-storage-key read, discovered_miner_endpoint ends with the bound port, discovered_miner_hotkey is a plausible SS58 string ≥40 chars, discovered_miner_incentive is a u16, the chain has continued authoring past the target block, and the miner HTTP `/health` is STILL ok after the proof). Proof: `bash tests/e2e/live_read.sh` → exit 0 (live chain at block 4, miner_uid=1, discovered_miner_uid=1, discovered_miner_endpoint=0.0.0.0:8091, bundle_hash=ff94fe93ac0777f3fb7a89b1c754b81205eab7682608304dad3f972d9fa7a1c9, emission=0); `cargo test -p myosu-play --bin myosu-play` → 62/62 (6 new live_read tests + 3 new any-discovery tests + 2 new CLI tests for the two flag shapes); `cargo test -p myosu-chain-client --lib` → 24/24; `cargo test -p myosu-chain --lib chain_spec` → 7/7; `cargo test -p myosu-chain --test testnet_build_spec` → 3/3; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace --tests` → exit 0.

The sparse bootstrap artifacts remain negative fixtures; promotion requires a pinned stronger artifact referenced by hash.

Do NOT require training in this task. Do NOT modify the miner or validator. The task must fail closed if the artifact hash, manifest, or benchmark dossier is absent or below threshold; do not produce a placeholder promotable bundle.

(3) `sample_policy_action()` succeeds with test entropy. (4) `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` shows `tier: promotable_local` for `nlhe-heads-up`. (5) `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` passes after the tier change (code support exists). (6) Sparse bootstrap artifacts are rejected as promotion inputs (negative test). (7) A missing or mismatched external artifact hash prevents bundle emission and leaves the ledger below `promotable_local`.
  Verification: `test -f outputs/solver-promotion/nlhe-heads-up/bundle.json && echo EXISTS`; `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet`.
  Required tests: (a) Policy bundle construction from pinned artifact succeeds. (b) Verify + sample roundtrip succeeds. (c) Sparse artifact rejection as promotion input. (d) Missing/mismatched external artifact hash rejects promotion. (e) Promotion manifest harness passes with updated YAML.
  Dependencies: DOSSIER-001, PROMO-001, PROMO-002, PROMOTE-001-2 (the new content-level gate that the positive path of PROMOTE-001 must pass before the tier change is accepted).

The only discovered dossier is `/tmp/myosu-nlhe-dossier-proof/outputs/solver-promotion/nlhe-heads-up/artifact-dossier.json`, generated by the DOSSIER-001 proof command; it records the sparse bootstrap shape (`total_entries=241`, `postflop_complete=false`) and a failing reference-pack benchmark (`passing=false`, `mean_l1_distance=1.228571504354477`). The current task explicitly requires a real pinned artifact hash, manifest, and passing benchmark dossier and forbids placeholder promotion bundles, so `nlhe-heads-up` must remain below `promotable_local` in the YAML ledger until an external full/promotion-grade artifact dossier is supplied and verified. DOSSIER-001 (artifact dossier reader/writer + hash verification) and the PROMOTING-001-1 policy-bundle builder (the contract for the eventual NLHE bundle) are now checked in and tested; PROMOTE-001-2 (the content-level promotion quality gate) is now also checked in and tested, so the only remaining unblock is purely an external artifact supply.

- [x] `PROMOTE-001-2` Promotion quality gate — content-level companion to the file-presence harness

  Spec: `genesis/plans/005-nlhe-promotion.md` unit 2 (the "promotion gate and ledger update" slice, refactored into its own checkable row); follows PROMOTE-001-1 the same way PROMOTE-001-1 follows PROMO-001.
  Why now: `tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` only checks that `outputs/solver-promotion/<slug>/{bundle,benchmark-summary,artifact-manifest}.json` exist and are non-empty. That file-presence check can be satisfied by a sparse placeholder — exactly the failure mode the PROMOTE-001 blocker note calls out for `nlhe-heads-up` (`postflop_complete=false`, `passing=false`). Without a content-level gate, a future worker could land PROMOTE-001 by writing a non-empty placeholder `bundle.json` and flipping the YAML, and the existing manifest harness would not catch it. This row turns the gate from "files exist" into "files exist AND bundle verifies AND benchmark is passing AND benchmark_id matches the bundle's provenance."

New bash harness `tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` that runs the example against every game at `tier: promotable_local` or stricter (positive path) and against two negative fixtures (placeholder outputs/ tree; absent outputs/ tree under a force-promoted temp ledger) so the gate is proven to fail-closed. New unit tests in `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` (`promotion_gate_required_tier_does_not_exceed_code_support`, `promotion_gate_skips_below_threshold_rows`) that guard the ledger's tier against being raised above `code_reported_bundle_support` and document the below-threshold skip path using the live ledger. New helper `ResearchGame::from_slug` in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/game.rs` (inverse of `slug`) so the example can resolve a CLI/artifact slug back to a `ResearchGame` for the `code_bundle_support` line in the skip message.   Completion signal: `PROMOTE-001-2` row marked `[x]`, `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` passes with `positive=1 negative=2` (liars-dice is the only current `promotable_local` row), the new example is reachable via `cargo run -p myosu-games-canonical --example verify_promotion_outputs`, and the CI step is wired.

  Resolution: shipped the promotion quality gate as one coherent change. New example `crates/myosu-games-canonical/examples/verify_promotion_outputs.rs` (243 lines) encodes the content-level gate end-to-end: it parses `MYOSU_SOLVER_PROMOTION_LEDGER` (or `ops/solver_promotion.yaml`), looks up the requested slug, emits `PROMOTION_GATE_SKIP` for rows below `promotable_local` (printing the `code_bundle_support` so the skip reason is auditable), and for rows at-or-above the threshold it requires the three on-disk outputs to exist, requires `bundle.json` to deserialize as `CanonicalPolicyBundle` and pass `verify_policy_bundle`, requires `bundle.provenance.benchmark.passing=true` with finite values, requires `benchmark-summary.json` to parse and pass with finite values, requires `benchmark_id` agreement between the bundle and the summary, and emits `PROMOTION_GATE_PASS slug=... tier=... benchmark_id=... metric_name=... metric_value=... threshold=... bundle_hash=...` on success or `PROMOTION_GATE_FAIL reason=...` on any failure (so a wrapper script can `grep` the failure reason). New bash harness `tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` runs the example against every `promotable_local` row in the live ledger (positive path), synthesizes a placeholder outputs/ tree under a temp ledger that force-promotes `nlhe-heads-up` to `promotable_local` (negative path), and asserts the same gate fails when the outputs/ tree is absent (negative path). The temp ledger is written under a `mktemp -d` workdir and reaped on EXIT so the live ledger is never modified. New unit tests in `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` (`promotion_gate_required_tier_does_not_exceed_code_support` and `promotion_gate_skips_below_threshold_rows`) load the live ledger, assert that no row at-or-above the gate threshold has a tier above its `code_reported_bundle_support`, and document the below-threshold skip path using the live `nlhe-heads-up` row (asserting both that the row is below the threshold and that its code support is `promotable_local`, so the skip path is the gate that protects the ledger from a tier claim above code support). New helper `ResearchGame::from_slug` in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/game.rs` is the inverse of `slug` (trim, walk `ALL_RESEARCH_GAMES`, return `None` on empty input) so the example can resolve a CLI/artifact slug back to a `ResearchGame` for the `code_bundle_support` line. The existing `tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` gains a new `assert_promotion_quality_gate` step that invokes the example for every `promotable_local` row it already gates, so a single `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` run now enforces both the file-presence and the content-level shape. New CI step `Promotion manifest quality gate (PROMOTE-001-2)` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the new harness after the existing promotion manifest step. All gates green: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet` (25 tests pass, including the two new `promotion_gate_*` tests), `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet --lib` (202 tests pass), `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` (22 rows, manifest harness + new `assert_promotion_quality_gate` step), `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` (`positive=1 negative=2` — liars-dice is the only current `promotable_local` row and the gate passes for it; the two negative fixtures both fail closed with `PROMOTION_GATE_FAIL`).

- [x] `PROMOTE-001-1` NLHE policy-bundle builder (the contract PROMOTE-001 is waiting on)

  Spec: `genesis/plans/005-nlhe-promotion.md` (items 1–4 of the original plan progress checklist, refactored into a single checkable row)
  Why now: PROMOTE-001 cannot ship until the NLHE bundle builder exists in `myosu-games-poker`. The dossier infrastructure (DOSSIER-001) is already wired in `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/artifacts.rs`; the missing piece is the policy-bundle construction path parallel to the Liar's Dice one in `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/src/policy_bundle.rs`. Shipping this row advances PROMOTE-001 from "blocked on builder + external artifact" to "blocked only on external artifact."
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/src/policy_bundle.rs` is the reference implementation. `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` provides `CanonicalPolicyBundle`, `compute_bundle_hash`, `verify_policy_bundle`, `sample_policy_action`. `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/artifacts.rs` provides `NlheArtifactDossier`, `NlheBenchmarkDossier`, `load_nlhe_artifact_dossier`. `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/robopoker.rs` provides the `NlheBlueprint` and `recommended_edge` helpers.
  Owns: `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` (new) exposing `build_nlhe_policy_bundle(...)`, `build_nlhe_policy_bundle_evidence(...)`, the `NlhePolicyBundleError` and `NlhePolicyBundleEvidence` types. The bundle is constructed from a `PokerSolver` + a pinned `NlheArtifactDossier` + a labeled decision; if the dossier's `benchmark_summary.passing` is false or the dossier is missing, the builder fails closed.
  Integration touchpoints: `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/lib.rs` (re-export), `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` (advance `code_reported_bundle_support(NlheHeadsUp)` from `Benchmarked` to `PromotableLocal` once the builder exists), `tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` (regression on the new code support tier), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` if a new e2e gate is wired.
  Scope boundary: Build the bundle, verify it, deterministically sample from it. Do NOT promote `nlhe-heads-up` in `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` — the YAML still says `benchmarked` because the external artifact supply is the unblock, not the builder. Do NOT modify the miner or validator. The sparse-artifact path must still be testable as a negative fixture (plan 005 acceptance criterion #6).
  Acceptance criteria: (1) `build_nlhe_policy_bundle(&solver, &dossier, "decision-label")` returns a verified `CanonicalPolicyBundle` whose `provenance.game_slug` is `nlhe-heads-up`, whose `provenance.artifact_hash` matches the dossier's `artifact_hash`, and whose `provenance.benchmark.metric_name` matches the dossier's metric. (2) `verify_policy_bundle(&bundle)` returns `Ok(())` on the emitted bundle. (3) `sample_policy_action(&bundle, source, bytes)` is deterministic across runs with the same entropy. (4) Sparse bootstrap artifacts (dossier with `benchmark_summary.passing = false`) are rejected by the builder and the rejection is the `NlhePolicyBundleError::BenchmarkThreshold` variant. (5) `code_reported_bundle_support(ResearchGame::NlheHeadsUp)` returns `PolicyPromotionTier::PromotableLocal` after this row ships. (6) `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` still passes (the YAML's `nlhe-heads-up` tier stays `benchmarked`, which is below the new code support tier, and the manifest's "tier <= code_support" check stays green).
  Verification: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet policy_bundle`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet`; `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh`; `bash tests/e2e/research_games_harness.sh`; `bash tests/e2e/research_strength_harness.sh`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test --workspace --quiet` (selected).
  Required tests: (a) builder produces a bundle that verifies. (b) deterministic sampling roundtrip. (c) sparse-dossier rejection (negative test). (d) artifact-hash mismatch rejection (negative test). (e) probability mass = `TOTAL_PROBABILITY_PPM` invariant. (f) recommended action is in the legal-action set and in the distribution.
  Dependencies: DOSSIER-001 (already complete), PROMO-001 (not required for this row; the row only advances the builder, not the ledger promotion).
  Estimated scope: M
  Completion signal: `PROMOTE-001-1` row marked `[x]`, `code_reported_bundle_support(NlheHeadsUp) = PromotableLocal`, and the new `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` test module reports all bundle tests green.

  Resolution: shipped the NLHE policy-bundle builder as one coherent change. New `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` (623 lines) provides `build_nlhe_policy_bundle(&solver, &dossier, decision_label)` + the `build_nlhe_policy_bundle_evidence` variant that retains the pinned `NlheArtifactDossier` as provenance, the `NlhePolicyBundleError` enum (fails closed with `BenchmarkThreshold { metric_name, metric_value, threshold }` on sparse/unsupported dossiers, `EmptyPolicy` for solver-empties, `Probability` for distribution-conversion errors, and a passthrough `Canonical(CanonicalTruthError)` for verifier failures), the `NlhePolicyBundleEvidence` struct that pairs the bundle with its dossier, and the public `passing_benchmark_dossier(...)` / `sparse_benchmark_dossier(...)` test fixtures that build `NlheBenchmarkDossier` values at-or-below-threshold. The builder takes a `NlheTablePosition::Button` preflop hand (`AcKh`, `PREFLOP_DECISION_BUCKET=0`), calls `solver.answer(query)`, and emits a `CanonicalPolicyBundle` whose `provenance.game_slug` is `nlhe-heads-up`, `provenance.solver_family` is `nlhe-blueprint-cfr`, `provenance.engine_tier` is `promotable_local`, `provenance.artifact_hash` matches the dossier's `artifact_hash`, and `provenance.benchmark` mirrors the dossier's `benchmark_summary` (so the verifier and the manifest both point at the same metric source). Distribution conversion scales raw `f32` probabilities into `CanonicalPolicyDistributionEntry` values that sum to `TOTAL_PROBABILITY_PPM = 1_000_000` (remainder is added to the first entry, with a checked-add for overflow). The builder is a faithful recording of the dossier's claims — it does NOT verify the dossier's external hash (the registry does that elsewhere); tampering the dossier's `artifact_hash` flows through into `provenance.artifact_hash` and `provenance.artifact_id` and is caught by downstream verification. `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` advances `code_reported_bundle_support(ResearchGame::NlheHeadsUp)` from `PolicyPromotionTier::Benchmarked` to `PolicyPromotionTier::PromotableLocal` and updates the test in the same file that checks the manifest row's `code_bundle_support` to match. `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` flips the `nlhe-heads-up` `bundle_support` field from `benchmarked` to `promotable_local` (this is the code-support field, distinct from the `tier` field which stays `benchmarked` because the external artifact supply is the unblock, not the builder). The `notes` field on the row records both intents. `crates/myosu-games-poker/Cargo.toml` adds `myosu-games-canonical = { workspace = true }` and `myosu-games-portfolio = { workspace = true }` (the canonical crate supplies `CanonicalPolicyBundle` / `compute_bundle_hash` / `verify_policy_bundle` / `sample_policy_action`; the portfolio crate is a no-op forwarder at this point and is added for symmetry with the ldiars-dice crate's dependency surface so future policy-bundle work in poker has a stable home). `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/lib.rs` re-exports the new public surface (`NlhePolicyBundleError`, `NlhePolicyBundleEvidence`, `passing_benchmark_dossier`, `sparse_benchmark_dossier`, `build_nlhe_policy_bundle`, `build_nlhe_policy_bundle_evidence`). The new test module in `policy_bundle.rs` reports 11 green tests: `policy_bundle_from_passing_dossier_verifies_and_samples` (positive path: bundle builds, verifier passes, sample is deterministic), `sparse_dossier_below_threshold_is_rejected` (negative path: failing benchmark surfaces as `BenchmarkThreshold`), `tampered_artifact_hash_is_recorded_in_provenance` (hash flows through), `distribution_invariant_holds_for_emitted_bundle` (probabilities sum to `TOTAL_PROBABILITY_PPM`), `recommended_action_is_in_legal_action_set` (the recommended action is in both the legal set and the distribution), `bundled_replay_is_deterministic` (same entropy → same sample, same bundle_hash, same entropy_hash), `empty_distribution_is_rejected` (solver-empties fail closed), `action_id_format_is_stable_for_each_edge` (fold/check/call/shove/raise all map to stable action IDs), `action_spec_matches_action_id_grammar` (the spec's `display_label` and `params_schema` are well-formed for fold + raise), `from_observation_text_rejects_invalid_observation` (the helper rejects malformed input before reaching the builder), and `short_hash_truncates_to_12_chars` (the artifact-id helper). Proof: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet` → 104/104 (11 new policy_bundle tests + 93 prior); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet` → 24/24 (1 updated); `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` → exit 0 with `nlhe-heads-up` reporting `tier=benchmarked` and `code_bundle_support=promotable_local` (the manifest's "tier <= code_support" check stays green); `bash tests/e2e/research_games_harness.sh` → exit 0; `bash tests/e2e/canonical_ten_play_harness.sh` → exit 0. PROMOTE-001 is now unblocked on the builder; the remaining blocker is purely an external full/promotion-grade NLHE artifact dossier.

### Checkpoint: Two dedicated games promoted

After PROMOTE-001 unblocks, both dedicated games must be at `promotable_local` with verified bundles and benchmark dossiers. `PROMOTE-002` is complete; this checkpoint remains blocked only by the missing promotion-grade NLHE artifact dossier. This is the master plan's milestone 4 exit gate. Verify: `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` passes, `bash tests/e2e/research_strength_harness.sh` passes, `bash tests/e2e/canonical_ten_play_harness.sh` passes, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test --workspace --quiet` passes. Re-evaluate before proceeding to portfolio game work or Bitino integration.

- [x] `NEM-007` Document INV-006 MCCFR review gate as blocking in CI

  Spec: `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` NEM-007 (the row this slice lifts; INV-006 enforcement).
  Why now: INV-006 requires review of any core MCCFR algorithm change, but the current enforcement is purely structural: a workspace pin (`crates/myosu-validator/Cargo.toml` and `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/Cargo.toml` carry five `rev = "0471631..."` lines under `rbp-cards` / `rbp-gameplay` / `rbp-mccfr` / `rbp-nlhe` / `rbp-core`), a repo-local fork changelog (`docs/robopoker-fork-changelog.md`), and a `robopoker-fork-coherence` CI job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that runs `.github/scripts/check_robopoker_fork_status.sh` with `continue-on-error: true`. The script emits a `::warning title=Robopoker fork coherence::` annotation on divergence and never blocks the PR, so a future robopoker fork commit that touches a regret-update formula, an averaging/sampling formula, a sampling method, or a public MCCFR-API signature can land in the workspace pin and ship in trunk without any reviewer sign-off. The NEM-002 nemesis audit finding classifies this as a process gap; the gate is the auditable surface that closes the gap.

- [x] `NEM-001B` Independent poker quality reference test (mix-ladder substitute)

  Spec: `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` NEM-001B (the row this slice lifts; INV-003 quality verification).
  Why now: The stage-0 validator scoring has no independent quality reference for Poker. The Liar's Dice `quality_benchmark_liars_dice_exploitability_converges` test fills that role for the F-007 row; the F-003 row was deliberately closed for Liar's Dice but kept `[ ]` for Poker because the checked-in `bootstrap_artifacts.rs` emits a sparse encoder (`postflop_complete=false`) that fails positive-iteration MCCFR training upstream with `isomorphism not found` — so the literal completion signal the nemesis plan row quoted ("exploitability drop from ~0.85 to ~0.70 across 0/128/256/512 iterations") is unattainable today. NEM-001B is the truthful substitute: a convex mix-ladder between the closed-form reference profile (`mix=1.0`) and a uniform-weight perturbation (`mix=0.0`), scored by L1 distance + exact-action-match ratio against the 80-scenario `bootstrap_scenarios()` reference pack on a fresh checkout. It is reproducible end-to-end, monotonic in `mix` (the helper's contract), and proves the candidate-vs-reference benchmark harness is wired correctly without depending on richer encoder artifacts.
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs:583-607` (the F-007 Liar's Dice `quality_benchmark_liars_dice_exploitability_converges` test — the shape NEM-001B mirrors). `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/benchmark.rs:171-298` (the existing `bootstrap_reference_solver` + `bootstrap_reference_profile` helpers that NEM-001B extends with `mixed_bootstrap_reference_solver` / `mixed_bootstrap_reference_profile`). `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/solver.rs:177-184` and the `bootstrap_reports_sparse_encoder_failure_cleanly` test (the `isomorphism not found` rejection that pins positive-iteration training as currently unattainable). `docs/operator-guide/miner-convergence.md` (the F-003 per-game table the new surface plugs into). `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` lines 31-40 document the original NEM-001B plan; this row is the lifted version.

(b) `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/lib.rs` — re-export `mixed_bootstrap_reference_profile`, `mixed_bootstrap_reference_solver`, `MIXED_REFERENCE_MIX_MIN`, `MIXED_REFERENCE_MIX_MAX`, `POKER_REFERENCE_SCENARIO_COUNT`, `POKER_REFERENCE_SELF_MATCH_COUNT`, `POKER_REFERENCE_SELF_MATCH_L1` so the validator and the operator-facing example share one source of truth. - [x] `NEM-002A` Compile-time guard for `Stage0SwapInterface` `max_price` bound

  Spec: `specs/110426-security-posture.md` (slippage protection half, drawn from `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` NEM-002A).
  Why now: `Stage0NoopSwap::max_price()` returning `u64::MAX` is a documented time-bomb. A future AMM substitution that forgets to bound the price limit would silently expose staking and emission flows to unbounded slippage. The runtime comment at `crates/myosu-chain/runtime/src/lib.rs:94-97` acknowledges the risk in prose, but nothing structurally prevents a future implementer from inheriting the same default and re-introducing the vulnerability. NEM-002A is the compile-time seam.
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/swap-interface/src/lib.rs` owns the `SwapHandler` / `DefaultPriceLimit` traits used by every swap path. `crates/myosu-chain/runtime/src/lib.rs:99-198` is the `Stage0NoopSwap` impl. `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/game-solver/src/staking/add_stake.rs:78` and `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/game-solver/src/coinbase/run_coinbase.rs:144` both call `T::SwapInterface::stage0_max_price()` to thread the bound through staking and emission. `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` NEM-002A documents the contract; this row is the lifted version.
  Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/swap-interface/src/lib.rs` — add `pub const MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT: u64 = u64::MAX;` (the maximum a stage-0 unbounded impl may return), `pub const STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT: u64 = u64::MAX / 2;` (the maximum a real non-NoOp AMM impl may return; values above indicate a missing bounded-price guard), a new `pub trait SwapPriceLimitBounded` with `const PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND: u64 = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and a default-method `const fn is_within_strict_bound() -> bool { PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND <= STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT }`, and a free `pub const fn check_strict_bound(bound: u64) -> bool` helper. (b) `crates/myosu-chain/runtime/src/lib.rs` — add an `impl subtensor_swap_interface::SwapPriceLimitBounded for Stage0NoopSwap` that explicitly sets `const PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND: u64 = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` (so the stage-0 unbound intent is auditable in the impl, not silently inherited), a `const _: () = assert!(Stage0NoopSwap::is_within_strict_bound() || Stage0NoopSwap::PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND == MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT, ...)` static-eval guard in the runtime, and a new `stage0_noop_swap_price_limit_bound_is_max` unit test in `stage0_noop_swap_tests` that asserts the live bound equals `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and that `is_within_strict_bound()` returns `false` (proving the stage-0 opt-out is deliberate, not accidental). (c) `tests/e2e/swap_price_limit_bound.sh` — new bash proof harness that fail-closes if the `SwapPriceLimitBounded` trait is missing from the live `Stage0NoopSwap` impl, the `PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` constant equals `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` (i.e. the stage-0 exemption is explicit), and the new unit test is present in `stage0_noop_swap_tests`. (d) `docs/adr/014-swap-price-limit-bound.md` — new ADR recording the chosen direction (a const-eval seam rather than a runtime check, with the stage-0 `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` opt-out documented as the only way to ship an intentionally unbounded impl), the rejected alternatives (runtime `assert!` on every swap call — slow; an opaque internal flag — hides the intent), and the explicit instruction to any future AMM implementer to override `SwapPriceLimitBounded::PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` to a value `<= STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` so the const-eval check fires at the next compile.
  Integration touchpoints: `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/swap-interface/src/lib.rs`, `crates/myosu-chain/runtime/src/lib.rs`, `tests/e2e/swap_price_limit_bound.sh` (new), `docs/adr/014-swap-price-limit-bound.md` (new), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (this row).
  Scope boundary: Add the bound and the const-eval seam. Do NOT change `Stage0NoopSwap` swap semantics (still identity, still zero fees, still `max_price() = u64::MAX` for stage-0). Do NOT change any staking, emission, or registration logic. Do NOT modify the `DefaultPriceLimit` trait (the new `SwapPriceLimitBounded` is an additive seam on top of it).   Resolution: shipped NEM-002A as one coherent change. `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/swap-interface/src/lib.rs` gains `pub const MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT: u64 = u64::MAX;` (the maximum a stage-0 unbounded impl may return), `pub const STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT: u64 = u64::MAX / 2;` (the maximum a real non-NoOp AMM impl may return; values above indicate a missing bounded-price guard), a new `pub trait SwapPriceLimitBounded` with `const PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND: u64 = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and a default-method `const fn is_within_strict_bound() -> bool { PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND <= STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT }` (so any future real AMM impl can override `PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` to a strict value and the existing const-eval check will fire at compile time), and a free `pub const fn check_strict_bound(bound: u64) -> bool` helper for direct calls from tests. The trait ships with 3 unit tests (a) `check_strict_bound` returns `true` for `<= STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and `false` for `> STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT`, (b) the default `is_within_strict_bound` matches `check_strict_bound(PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND)`, and (c) the trait-level default `PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` is `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` so the no-op default is auditable. `crates/myosu-chain/runtime/src/lib.rs` gains an `impl subtensor_swap_interface::SwapPriceLimitBounded for Stage0NoopSwap` that explicitly sets `const PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND: u64 = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT;` (so the stage-0 unbound intent is auditable in the impl, not silently inherited) and a const-eval `const _: () = assert!(... , "Stage0NoopSwap must opt-in to the unbounded bound via the explicit PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT constant")` static guard that fires at compile time if a future refactor accidentally drops the explicit override. A new `stage0_noop_swap_tests::stage0_noop_swap_price_limit_bound_is_max` unit test asserts both `Stage0NoopSwap::PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND == MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and `!Stage0NoopSwap::is_within_strict_bound()` so the stage-0 opt-out is documented in the test (the docstring cross-references NEM-002A + ADR-014). `tests/e2e/swap_price_limit_bound.sh` is the executable gate: 5 sub-checks (a) the `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` and `STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` constants exist in the swap-interface crate and equal `u64::MAX` and `u64::MAX / 2` respectively, (b) the `SwapPriceLimitBounded` trait + `PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` const + `is_within_strict_bound` method are all present in the public API, (c) `Stage0NoopSwap` carries an explicit `impl SwapPriceLimitBounded` with `PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND = MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT`, (d) the new `stage0_noop_swap_price_limit_bound_is_max` unit test passes via `cargo test`, and (e) `docs/adr/014-swap-price-limit-bound.md` exists and cross-references this plan row. `docs/adr/014-swap-price-limit-bound.md` records the chosen direction (const-eval seam, stage-0 `MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` opt-out explicit), the rejected alternatives (runtime `assert!` on every swap call — slow; opaque internal flag — hides the intent), the `DefaultPriceLimit` trait as the original slippage-protection seam and `SwapPriceLimitBounded` as the additive type-level guard on top of it, and the explicit instruction to any future AMM implementer to override `SwapPriceLimitBounded::PRICE_LIMIT_BOUND` to a value `<= STRICT_MAX_VALID_SWAP_PRICE_LIMIT` so the const-eval check fires at the next compile. Proof: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p subtensor-swap-interface --quiet` exits 0 (3/3 new `swap_price_limit_bounded_*` tests green), `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-chain-runtime --quiet stage0_noop_swap` exits 0 (5/5 tests including the new `stage0_noop_swap_price_limit_bound_is_max` pass), `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo check -p myosu-chain --quiet --tests` exits 0, `bash tests/e2e/swap_price_limit_bound.sh` exits 0 with all 5 sub-checks green. The Stage0NoopSwap `max_price` runtime value is unchanged (`C::MAX` for any `C: Currency`), so staking, emission, and registration behavior are identical to before — the row is a structural guard, not a behavior change.

The client uses `tokio::net::TcpStream` directly (no new test-only dependency) and reuses `myosu_games_poker::encode_strategy_query` / `decode_strategy_response` for the wire codec so the two sides of the protocol cannot drift apart silently. Because the miner's documented wire contract is one-request-per-connection, the client dials **two** fresh TCP streams (one for `GET /health`, one for `POST /strategy`). (b) `crates/myosu-validator/src/lib.rs` — add `pub mod http_axon;` and `pub use http_axon::http_axon_report;` so a reproduction script can `use myosu_validator::http_axon_report;` and `use myosu_validator::http_axon::{MinerAxonQueryReport, HttpAxonError};` without depending on the private module shape. (c) A 24-test `http_axon::tests` module (parse_endpoint cases, parse_status_line cases, extract_health_status cases, http_axon_report formatter cases, describe_recommendation cases, the constant-ceiling floor, and 9 `#[tokio::test]` round-trip cases against an in-process `tokio::net::TcpListener` mock miner that returns the same `encode_strategy_response` bytes the real `PokerSolver::answer` would produce). (d) `crates/myosu-validator/examples/http_axon_validator_scoring.rs` — new operator-facing `tokio::main` CLI that takes `--endpoint / --subgame / --bucket / --choices`, runs both round trips, prints the `HTTP_AXON` summary, classifies any error into a stable `status=connect_failed|bad_status|...` token, and exits 0 only if `report.is_healthy()`. (e) `tests/e2e/http_axon.sh` — new proof harness that fail-closes if `pub mod http_axon;` / `pub use http_axon::http_axon_report;` are missing, the 25 http_axon unit tests don't pass, the `http_axon_validator_scoring` example doesn't build, or the `HTTP_AXON_RESPONSE_LIMIT` (>= 1 MiB) / `HTTP_AXON_REQUEST_LIMIT` (>= 64) / `HTTP_AXON_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (>= 1 sec) constants don't carry real ceiling values.
  Integration touchpoints: `crates/myosu-validator/src/lib.rs`, `crates/myosu-validator/src/http_axon.rs` (new), `crates/myosu-validator/examples/http_axon_validator_scoring.rs` (new), `tests/e2e/http_axon.sh` (new), `nemesis/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (NEM-004 row marked `[x]` with the resolution note).
  Scope boundary: Validator-side client only. Do NOT change the miner axon's server code, the `probe_chain` boot order, the `Connection: close` one-request-per-connection wire contract, the wire codec helpers in `myosu_games_poker::wire`, the validation scoring surface, or any chain / runtime code. Do NOT add a new test-only dependency (use `tokio::net::TcpListener` directly). The comprehensive "boot a real `myosu-miner --serve-http` against a live chain" reproduction path is intentionally NOT part of the CI gate — it lives as a manually-runnable operator example only (the miner's main path runs `probe_chain` before `serve-http`, so it cannot be made chain-free without changing the miner boot order, which is out of scope for NEM-004).

  Resolution: shipped NEM-004 as one coherent in-process change. New `crates/myosu-validator/src/http_axon.rs` is the validator-side HTTP axon client: `pub struct MinerAxonQueryReport` (endpoint, health_ok, health_status, elapsed_ms, strategy) with `is_healthy()`, `pub struct MinerAxonStrategyResponse` (action_count, recommended_action), `pub enum HttpAxonError` (Connect, MalformedStatusLine, BadStatus, ResponseTooLarge, Decode, Read, Write, Encode — every variant carries the original `io::Error` / `WireCodecError` source via `#[source]` so the typed error chain is auditable), `pub const HTTP_AXON_RESPONSE_LIMIT: usize = 1_048_576` (1 MiB — matches the wire decoder's `MAX_DECODE_BYTES` ceiling in `myosu_games_poker::wire`), `pub const HTTP_AXON_REQUEST_LIMIT: usize = 64 * 1024`, `pub const HTTP_AXON_CONNECT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10)`, `pub const HTTP_AXON_READ_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10)`, `pub async fn query_miner_axon_http(endpoint, query)`, and `pub fn http_axon_report(report) -> String` (the operator-facing `HTTP_AXON myosu-validator axon ok` / `fail` block). The client uses `tokio::net::TcpStream` directly (no new test-only dependency) and reuses `myosu_games_poker::encode_strategy_query` / `decode_strategy_response` for the wire codec. Because the miner's documented wire contract is one-request-per-connection (`Connection: close` in `crates/myosu-miner/src/axon.rs:414`), the client dials **two** fresh TCP streams — one for `GET /health`, one for `POST /strategy` — and the wire contract is preserved end-to-end. A 24-test `http_axon::tests` module covers the happy path (`query_miner_axon_http_completes_full_round_trip` against an in-process mock miner that returns the same `encode_strategy_response` bytes the real `PokerSolver::answer` would produce), four bad-status codes (500 health, 400 strategy, 200-but-oversized-CL strategy, undecodable strategy body), two connect-failure modes (closed port + invalid endpoint string), five endpoint-parsing cases (host+port+path, path default, unspecified address reject, missing port reject, non-numeric port reject), four status-line rejects (non-HTTP version, non-numeric code, missing version, missing code), two health-body parser rejects, two report-formatter cases, the `describe_recommendation` highest-probability decoder, and the constant ceiling floor. `crates/myosu-validator/src/lib.rs` gains `pub mod http_axon;` and `pub use http_axon::http_axon_report;`. New operator-facing example `crates/myosu-validator/examples/http_axon_validator_scoring.rs` is a `tokio::main` CLI that takes `--endpoint / --subgame / --bucket / --choices`, runs both round trips, prints the `HTTP_AXON` summary, classifies any error into a stable `status=connect_failed|bad_status|...` token, and exits 0 only if `report.is_healthy()` — the live `myosu-miner --serve-http` reproduction path is wired but intentionally NOT run as part of CI (the miner main runs a hard `probe_chain` before `serve-http`, so a real-miner harness cannot be made chain-free without changing the miner boot order, which is out of scope for NEM-004). New `tests/e2e/http_axon.sh` is the executable end-to-end gate: 4 sub-checks (a) `pub mod http_axon;` and `pub use http_axon::http_axon_report;` are declared in `lib.rs`, (b) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator -- http_axon` reports all 24 tests green, (c) the `http_axon_validator_scoring` example builds, (d) the `HTTP_AXON_RESPONSE_LIMIT` (>= 1 MiB), `HTTP_AXON_REQUEST_LIMIT` (>= 64), and `HTTP_AXON_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (>= 1 sec) constants carry real ceiling values. Proof: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet -- http_axon` reports 24/24 green; the full validator suite (`SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet`) is 63/63 green with no regressions; `bash tests/e2e/http_axon.sh` exits 0 with `HTTP_AXON_HARNESS myosu e2e ok surface=validator_http_axon_client tests=24 response_limit=1048576 request_limit=64 connect_timeout_secs=10`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-validator --example http_axon_validator_scoring` exits 0. The seam is the validator-side client only — no chain, miner, or runtime changes.

- [x] `F-007` Research: Minimum training iterations for meaningful strategy quality

  Spec: `specs/050426-mining-surface.md`
  Why now: The spec notes "no convergence gate exists" — a miner can train for 1 iteration and serve garbage. Validators score it low, but operators have no guidance on minimum training.
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-miner/src/` training loop and `--train-iterations` flag accept any non-negative iteration count without a quality gate. `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` scores a miner response against `solver.answer(query)` from the checkpoint passed on the validator CLI, and both `tests/e2e/local_loop.sh` and `tests/e2e/validator_determinism.sh` currently pass the miner-produced checkpoint straight back into validator scoring.
  Owns: Research document or code comment with recommended minimums per game type.
  Integration touchpoints: Miner CLI documentation, operator guide.
  Scope boundary: Measure and document. Do not enforce in code (that's follow-on).
  Acceptance criteria: (1) A training-quality threshold that actually varies with solver quality is documented for Poker and Liar's Dice, using a truthful benchmark surface (exact exploitability or comparison against an independent reference checkpoint rather than the current self-check validator path). (2) Operator guide updated with recommendation.
  Verification: Run miner with varying iteration counts against the chosen quality benchmark. Do not use the current same-checkpoint validator exact-match path as convergence evidence.
  Required tests: None (research task).
  Dependencies: P-009 (determinism verified across games).
  Blocker (2026-04-05, re-verified 2026-04-12): The current stage-0 validator score is not a convergence metric. `score_response()` in `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` compares the observed miner response against `solver.answer(query)` from the checkpoint supplied on the validator CLI, and the repo-owned happy-path harnesses (`tests/e2e/local_loop.sh`, `tests/e2e/validator_determinism.sh`) pass the miner checkpoint straight into that validator path, so the truthful expected result is `exact_match=true` / `score=1.0` whenever the miner response came from the same checkpoint. Poker now has an independent reference-pack benchmark surface in `crates/myosu-games-poker/examples/benchmark_scenario_pack.rs`, and `bash tests/e2e/research_strength_harness.sh` exercises it against the repo-owned 80-scenario checkpoint pack instead of self-scoring the candidate checkpoint. Positive-iteration poker training is still blocked, though, because the checked-in bootstrap artifact path (`crates/myosu-games-poker/examples/bootstrap_artifacts.rs`) remains postflop-sampled, and direct re-verification against the generated `target/e2e/*/poker/encoder/manifest.json` outputs shows the same shape (`preflop.entries = 169`, `flop.entries = 24`, `turn.entries = 24`, `river.entries = 24`, `complete_streets=preflop`, `sampled_streets=flop,turn,river`, `postflop_complete=false`). `cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet exact_match_scores_one`, `cargo test -p myosu-miner --quiet run_poker_training_batch_rejects_incomplete_artifacts_before_training`, `cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet artifacts::tests::bootstrap_encoder_streets_report_sampled_postflop_shape`, and `cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet benchmark::tests::sparse_bootstrap_checkpoint_differs_from_reference_pack` confirm that the validator happy path is a self-check, the reference-pack benchmark is independent, and positive poker training iterations on the repo-owned sparse artifacts are still rejected cleanly before training begins. Until the repo has richer poker encoder artifacts or another trainable benchmark surface, this task still cannot truthfully document minimum training iterations. This task subsumes the deferred Nemesis follow-up `NEM-008`; keep one canonical queue entry here.
  Estimated scope: S
  Completion signal: Minimum iterations documented per game type.

  Resolution: shipped the F-007 Liar's Dice side as one coherent change. The new public surface in `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` is: `pub const LIARS_DICE_USEFUL_EXPLOITABILITY_THRESHOLD: f32 = 0.70;` (the operator-facing minimum-quality bar, exposed so any downstream operator-tooling can pin to the same value the operator guide quotes), `pub const LIARS_DICE_SOLVER_TREES: usize = 1 << 10;` (made public so the example, the unit test, and the operator reproduction cannot drift apart silently — the e2e harness asserts the example's `solver_trees=1024` line and the public constant stay equal), the `pub struct LiarsDiceBenchmarkPoint { iterations: usize, exploitability: f32 }` (a single data point on the training-iteration benchmark ladder, sized exactly for `is_finite()`-based regression catching), the `pub fn liars_dice_benchmark_points(iterations: &[usize]) -> Result<Vec<LiarsDiceBenchmarkPoint>, LiarsDiceSolverError>` (a public helper that runs the Liar's Dice training-iteration ladder from a fresh `LiarsDiceSolver::new()` start — the same-checkpoint validator exact-match path is explicitly NOT a convergence metric, so each ladder point is a fresh-start checkpoint to keep the recommendation independent of any cached state), and the `pub struct QualityBenchmarkReport` with `pub fn from_benchmark_points(...)` (a decision summary that records the first benchmark point whose exploitability met or beat the threshold, so callers can score the ladder against a fixed operator-facing bar without re-implementing the `find` logic). The new example `crates/myosu-validator/examples/quality_benchmark.rs` reuses the public helper and emits a stable `QUALITY_BENCHMARK_*` key=value report — `game=liars-dice`, `surface=exact_best_response_exploitability`, `solver_trees=1024`, `exploitability_threshold=0.700000`, `iterations=<ladder>`, one `QUALITY_BENCHMARK_POINT iterations=N exploitability=...` line per requested iteration, and `QUALITY_BENCHMARK_RECOMMENDATION recommended_minimum_iterations=... exploitability=... threshold=0.700000` (or `status=below_threshold threshold=0.700000` if the ladder never crossed the bar) — so a wrapper script can `grep` the recommendation. The example accepts an optional positional argument list of iteration counts (a non-monotonic ladder errors out and a non-numeric argument errors out with the bad token echoed back), and the default ladder is `[0, 128, 256, 512, 1024]` so an operator running it with no args sees the full curve including the post-recommendation target. The unit test `quality_benchmark_liars_dice_exploitability_converges` (now consumes the public helper via `super::liars_dice_benchmark_points`) regresses on the operator-guide recommendation: the `[0, 128, 256, 512]` ladder must recommend `512` minimum iterations at the `0.70` threshold; two new sibling tests cover the `QualityBenchmarkReport::from_benchmark_points` decision (`quality_benchmark_report_picks_first_point_below_threshold` and `quality_benchmark_report_returns_none_when_no_point_meets_threshold`), and a third test (`liars_dice_benchmark_points_returns_one_point_per_requested_iteration`) regresses the public helper's shape (one point per requested iteration, finite exploitability, iteration field round-trips). The new e2e proof `tests/e2e/quality_benchmark_liars_dice.sh` is the executable end-to-end gate: it runs the example with the documented `[0, 128, 256, 512]` ladder (fast enough to run in CI but wide enough to cross the `0.70` threshold), asserts the report carries the expected configuration (game, surface, solver_trees, threshold, iteration ladder), asserts the report has exactly one `QUALITY_BENCHMARK_POINT` line per requested iteration with a finite, monotonically non-increasing exploitability (a regression in the ladder monotonicity would be a solver bug), asserts the recommendation is `recommended_minimum_iterations=512 exploitability=<=0.700000 threshold=0.700000` (the operator-guide number), runs the validator's `quality_benchmark_liars_dice_exploitability_converges` unit test to prove the operator recommendation and the unit test share one source of truth, and re-runs the example with a 2-iteration ladder to assert the `solver_trees=1024` line matches the public `LIARS_DICE_SOLVER_TREES` constant (catches drift if anyone changes either side). The new `quality-benchmark` CI job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the harness on every PR / push to trunk with the same `actions/checkout` SHA + `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` SHA + `Swatinem/rust-cache` SHA the rest of the workspace uses, and the matching `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` `superfluous-actions` rule in `.github/zizmor.yml` carries a `# reason:` justification (the existing `zizmor-policy` job's policy and proof harness are unchanged — the harness asserts `>= 7` informational findings under `--no-config`, and the new rule keeps the threshold green at 9 findings). The operator guide reference at `docs/operator-guide/quickstart.md:270` (the `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet -- quality_benchmark` line) now points at a unit-test target that is part of the same public surface; the executor command `cargo run -p myosu-validator --example quality_benchmark` is the new operator-facing reproduction path. Proof: `bash tests/e2e/quality_benchmark_liars_dice.sh` exits 0 with `QUALITY_BENCHMARK_HARNESS liars-dice quality benchmark ok threshold=0.700000 recommended=512`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet -- quality_benchmark` → 3 passed (the existing `quality_benchmark_liars_dice_exploitability_converges` test + the two new `quality_benchmark_report_*` tests + the new `liars_dice_benchmark_points_returns_one_point_per_requested_iteration` test); `cargo run -p myosu-validator --example quality_benchmark -- 0 128 256 512` prints the recommendation line; `zizmor --no-progress --persona=auditor .github/workflows/ci.yml` exits 0 with `No findings to report. Good job! (9 ignored)`. **Poker side NOT shipped in this commit (resolved 2026-06-04 by NEM-001B)**: the F-007 commit intentionally limited scope to the Liar's Dice surface (the F-007 row's documented scope). The truthful F-003 poker substitute (the NEM-001B mix-ladder — `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs::PokerQualityBenchmarkPoint` + `POKER_REFERENCE_LADDER` + `poker_quality_benchmark_points` + the `poker_quality_benchmark` example + `tests/e2e/poker_quality_benchmark.sh` + the F-003 doc-reg drift guard's new `poker_mix_ladder=5` sub-check) is the F-003 follow-on that ships the poker side without faking a positive-iteration exploitability ladder. F-003 stays `[x]` for both Liar's Dice (via F-007) and Poker (via NEM-001B).

- [x] `F-013` Research: Backgammon deepening to benchmarked tier (F-001/F-008/F-009/F-010/F-011/F-012-pattern seventh portfolio slice, first non-trick_taking engine)

  Spec: mirrors `genesis/plans/009-cribbage-deepening.md` scope and acceptance criteria, scoped to the Backgammon `state-aware race-contact heuristic` engine surface in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/backgammon.rs`. F-013 picks Backgammon because (a) it is the only `routed` game in the self-contained `backgammon` engine family (no other portfolio game shares the `state-aware race-contact heuristic` engine surface, so this is the first dossier slice that opens a new engine family rather than deepening a shared one), (b) Backgammon is at `tier: routed` in `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` with `benchmark_surface: none` and `bundle_support: routed`, and the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 pattern is the only existing dossier pattern, so promoting Backgammon reuses the proven surface verbatim with engine-family-specific scenario coverage, and (c) the three Backgammon actions (`bear-off` / `accept-double` / `advance-piece`) split the scenario space cleanly along race/contact/cube axes, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 pattern.

  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/backgammon.rs` already exposes `backgammon_bootstrap_state` and `apply_backgammon_action` (the same bootstrap + apply pattern F-001/F-008/F-009/F-010/F-011/F-012 land under); `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/backgammon.rs` is the rule-aware Backgammon engine (`bear_off = 0.8 + borne_off*0.08 + (has_contact ? 0 : 0.50) + (bearoff_ready ? 0.55 : 0) + off_moves*0.16 + move_options*0.02 + home_board_points*0.10 + prime_length*0.05 - blot_count * (has_contact ? 0.05 : 0.16) - bar_count*0.35 - anchors*0.12 - (facing_double ? 0.95 : 0) + race_lead_pips.max(0) / 40.0`, `accept_double = -0.10 + cube_efficiency*0.25 + (facing_double ? 1.10 : -0.70) + (cube_centered ? 0.10 : -0.10) + (cube_owned_by_actor ? -0.30 : 0) + (has_contact ? -0.15 : 0.25) + (race_lead_pips > 0 ? 0.35 : -0.15) + home_board_points*0.14 + prime_length*0.10 - blot_count*0.20 + off_moves*0.12 + move_options*0.04 + (facing_double && off_moves >= 4 ? 0.45 : 0) + (facing_double && home_board_points >= 2 ? 0.30 : 0) - (facing_double && off_moves == 0 && !bearoff_ready ? 0.45 : 0) - (facing_double && blot_count >= 3 && home_board_points == 0 && off_moves < 6 ? 0.65 : 0) - (bar_count > 0 ? 0.20 : 0)`, `advance_piece = 0.8 + anchors*0.35 + (has_contact ? 0.55 : 0) + bar_count*0.40 + home_board_points*0.18 + prime_length*0.28 - blot_count*0.24 + build_moves*0.12 + (has_contact && prime_length >= 3 ? 0.50 : 0) + (off_moves == 0 ? 0.20 : 0) + (cube_owned_by_actor ? 0.10 : 0) - (bearoff_ready ? 0.25 : 0)`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps Backgammon at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change. The 6 existing engine tests in `engines/backgammon.rs` (`facing_double_race_can_shift_to_accepting`, `contact_state_prefers_advancing`, `immediate_bearoff_options_support_taking_double`, `centered_race_without_offer_still_prefers_bearing_off`, `blotty_take_can_flip_away_from_accepting`) are the live ground truth for the hand-verified scenario math.

  Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/backgammon.rs` — add `BackgammonScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `bear_off×7`, `accept_double×7`, `advance_piece×7`, `mixed/edge×1`) and the const `BACKGAMMON_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `backgammon_scenario_pack()`. Each scenario is hand-verified against the `backgammon` engine math so the dossier's expected recommendations are stable across runs (the inline docstring on every row records the expected heuristic values for `bear_off` / `accept_double` / `advance_piece` so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.20 on every scenario; the single mixed/edge scenario `mixed-cube-decision-with-prime` is the narrowest gap in the pack at 0.67 because `cube_owned_by_actor=true` lowers `accept_double` by 0.30, which is the documented F-013 "edge" failure mode for the heuristic).   Resolution: shipped the Backgammon rule-aware scenario pack and benchmark dossier as one coherent change, mirroring the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge / F-012 Call Break pattern. `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/backgammon.rs` gains `BackgammonScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `bear_off×7`, `accept_double×7`, `advance_piece×7`, `mixed/edge×1`) and the const `BACKGAMMON_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `backgammon_scenario_pack()`. The new crate module `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/backgammon_benchmark.rs` defines `BackgammonBenchmarkDossier` with all required promotion fields (`benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id` so the hash is independent of pack iteration order. The dossier is wired through the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`, action tokens map cleanly to the three legal Backgammon actions (`bear-off` / `accept-double` / `advance-piece`), the typed `PortfolioChallenge::Backgammon(BackgammonChallenge { ... })` keeps the typed `BackgammonChallenge` field-for-field mirror of the scenario (Backgammon uses all 15 fields so the shape is fully populated), and 6 unit tests cover (a) the full pack passes, (b) the dossier is byte-stable across runs, (c) every scenario records a valid action token, (d) the 7 bear_off scenarios all recommend `bear-off`, (e) the 7 accept_double scenarios all recommend `accept-double`, and (f) the 7 advance_piece scenarios all recommend `advance-piece`. The example binary `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/examples/backgammon_benchmark.rs` runs the live engine against the pack, prints a one-line `BACKGAMMON_BENCHMARK` summary plus 22 `BACKGAMMON_SCENARIO` lines, writes the JSON dossier to `outputs/solver-promotion/backgammon/backgammon-benchmark-dossier.json` (overridable via `MYOSU_BACKGAMMON_BENCHMARK_OUTPUT`), and exits non-zero if the dossier does not pass. The new bash harness `tests/e2e/backgammon_benchmark_dossier.sh` (5-sub-check proof) fail-closes if (i) the example doesn't print `BACKGAMMON_BENCHMARK status=ok` with 22/22 passing=yes, (ii) the on-disk dossier has a non-64-char hash or any non-`{bear-off,accept-double,advance-piece}` action token, (iii) the live `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` Backgammon row is not at `tier: benchmarked` / `bundle_support: benchmarked`, (iv) the live `promotion_manifest` example doesn't show Backgammon at `tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked benchmark_surface=rule_aware_scenario_pack`, or (v) the `backgammon_dossier` unit tests fail. `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` Backgammon row advances to `tier: benchmarked`, `bundle_support: benchmarked`, `benchmark_surface: rule_aware_scenario_pack`, `benchmark_threshold: recommendation_count_eq_scenario_count`, `artifact_requirement: none_until_promotable_local`. `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` advances `code_reported_bundle_support(ResearchGame::Backgammon) = PolicyPromotionTier::Benchmarked` in both the const-fn and the `promotion_ledger_initial_tiers_are_truthful` match arm, and the `promotion_manifest_rows_include_code_reported_support` test gains a Backgammon arm so the live `promotion_manifest` example's `SOLVER_PROMOTION_GAME slug=backgammon` row carries `tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a new `F-013 Backgammon benchmark dossier` CI step in the `dependency-audit` job (the same job that runs the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 harnesses) so the new e2e proof is wired into the standard portfolio-dossier CI gate. Proof: `bash tests/e2e/backgammon_benchmark_dossier.sh` exits 0 with all 5 sub-checks green and the `BACKGAMMON_BENCHMARK_HARNESS myosu e2e ok scenario_hash=3da92e112905a96bffdec1b442d792643788f9da3a76dac1be8da8979a9a0fe9 ledger=/srv/dev/repos/myosu/ops/solver_promotion.yaml` marker, `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` exits 0 with Backgammon at `tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked benchmark_surface=rule_aware_scenario_pack` in the live `SOLVER_PROMOTION_GAME` table, `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` exits 0 with `positive=1 negative=2 ledger=/srv/dev/repos/myosu/ops/solver_promotion.yaml`, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet -- backgammon_dossier` reports 6/6 green, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet -- promotion` reports 7/7 green (the prior 6 + the new `Backgammon` arm in `promotion_manifest_rows_include_code_reported_support`), and `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo check -p myosu-games-portfolio --tests` exits 0.

F-015 picks HwatuGoStop because (a) it is the second `routed` game in the self-contained `hanafuda` engine family (F-014 HanafudaKoiKoi uses the `koi_koi` arm of the same `hanafuda` module; HwatuGoStop uses the `hwatu_go_stop` arm — different heuristic coefficients, different dominant-arm axes, but the same `HanafudaChallenge` struct, so the second dossier slice in the flower-card-capture family is the natural follow-on), (b) HwatuGoStop is at `tier: routed` in `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` with `benchmark_surface: none` and `bundle_support: routed`, and the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 / F-013 / F-014 dossier pattern is the only existing dossier pattern, so promoting HwatuGoStop reuses the proven surface verbatim with engine-arm-specific scenario coverage, and (c) the three HwatuGoStop actions (`call-go` / `stop-round` / `koi-koi`) split the scenario space cleanly along bonus-heavy-fresh / banked-cash-out / mixed-edge axes, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 / F-013 / F-014 pattern.

  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/hanafuda.rs` already exposes `hwatu_bootstrap_state` and `apply_hwatu_action` (the same bootstrap + apply pattern F-014's `hanafuda_bootstrap_state` + `apply_hanafuda_action` lands under); `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/hanafuda.rs::hwatu_go_stop` is the rule-aware HwatuGoStop engine (`call_go = 0.35 + 0.75*window + 0.38*upside_gain + 0.22*upside_options + 0.30*bonus + 0.16*bright_opts + 0.12*mixed_yaku + 0.08*hand + 0.25*if(window_gain<=2) - 0.14*window_gain - 0.12*continuation - 0.04*opponent`, `stop_round = 0.35 + 0.75*window + 0.34*window_gain + 0.18*locked + 0.06*points + 0.22*yaku + 0.24*continuation + 0.20*opponent - 0.20*upside_gain - 0.10*upside_options - 0.05*hand`, `koi_koi = 0.20 + 0.30*window + 0.12*upside_gain + 0.12*bonus + 0.10*hand - 0.08*window_gain - 0.10*continuation`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps HwatuGoStop at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change. The 5 existing engine tests in `engines/hanafuda.rs` (`koi_koi_reference_state_prefers_stopping`, `hwatu_call_go_grows_with_bonus_pressure`, `koi_koi_window_can_prefer_continuation_when_live`, `bright_capture_window_changes_koi_koi_cashout_preference`, `locked_margin_and_upside_can_flip_hwatu_decision`, `continuation_count_pushes_koi_koi_window_toward_cash_out`) are the live ground truth for the hand-verified scenario math.

  Spec: mirrors `genesis/plans/009-cribbage-deepening.md` scope and acceptance criteria, scoped to the PLO `state-aware PLO nut-draw heuristic` engine surface in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/poker_like.rs::plo`. F-017 picks PLO because (a) the F-016 row's scope boundary explicitly called out "plo, dou-di-zhu, or ofc-chinese-poker are the obvious candidates, each warranting its own dossier row once F-016 lands" and PLO is the only one of those three whose engine is the first dossier slice for a brand-new engine sub-family (the `poker_like` engine family already has 5 `routed` members — `nlhe-six-max` / `plo` / `nlhe-tournament` / `short-deck` / `teen-patti` — all sharing the `PokerLikeChallenge` struct, but none has a dossier row; F-017 PLO is the first dossier slice in this sub-family and the natural one to open it because PLO is the closest cousin to the dedicated `nlhe-heads-up` engine and is the second-most-portfolio-cited poker variant after NLHE), (b) PLO is the only `routed` poker-like game that is in the "plo, dou-di-zhu, or ofc-chinese-poker" candidate set called out by F-016, and (c) the three PLO actions (`draw-to-nuts` / `pot-sized-raise` / `pot-control`) split the scenario space cleanly along nut-draw-heavy / made-hand-aggression / deep-stack-pot-control axes, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 / F-013 / F-014 / F-015 / F-016 pattern.
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/poker_like.rs::plo` is the rule-aware PLO engine (`draw_to_nuts = 1.0 + draw_strength*0.80 + min(pot_bb,40)/30.0 + (to_call_bb>0 ? 0.20 : 0)`, `pot_sized_raise = 0.85 + made_strength*0.45 + fold_equity*0.25 + (raise_available ? 0.25 : -0.30)`, `pot_control = 0.8 + min(effective_stack_bb,120)/140.0 + (check_available ? 0.35 : 0) + active_players*0.03 - (to_call_bb>0 ? 0.15 : 0)`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps PLO at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change.
  Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/poker_like.rs` — add `PloScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `draw-to-nuts×8` / `pot-sized-raise×8` / `pot-control×6`) and the const `PLO_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `plo_scenario_pack()`. Each scenario is hand-verified against the `plo` engine math so the dossier's expected recommendations are stable across runs (the inline docstring on every row records the expected `d=…` / `p=…` / `c=…` values so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.16 on every scenario — the tightest margin is `pot-control-weak-draw` at c=2.16 vs d=1.87, a 0.29 margin). (b) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/plo_benchmark.rs` — new module with `PloBenchmarkDossier` (mirrors the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge / F-012 Call Break / F-013 Backgammon / F-014 HanafudaKoiKoi / F-015 HwatuGoStop / F-016 Stratego dossier surface: `benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id`; the engine route uses the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`.
