F-018 picks Dou-Di-Zhu because (a) the F-016 row's scope boundary explicitly called out "plo, dou-di-zhu, or ofc-chinese-poker are the obvious candidates" and the F-017 row's scope boundary named `dou-di-zhu` as the obvious next shedding-family candidate once the `poker_like` engine sub-family dossier row shipped, (b) Dou-Di-Zhu is the only `routed` shedding-family game that the F-016 / F-017 scope boundaries explicitly named — the `shedding` engine family has 3 `routed` members (`dou-di-zhu` / `pusoy-dos` / `tien-len`) all sharing the `SheddingChallenge` struct, but none has a dossier row; F-018 Dou-Di-Zhu is the first dossier slice in this sub-family and the natural one to open it because Dou-Di-Zhu is the 3-player variant with the richest engine surface (12 feature fields vs the 7-field PLO surface F-017 just opened) and the only shedding variant that distinguishes bomb-preservation as a top-level ranked arm, and (c) the three Dou-Di-Zhu actions (`preserve-bomb` / `landlord-bid` / `shed-lowest`) split the scenario space cleanly along bomb-availability / on-the-lead-control / finishing-line axes, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 / F-013 / F-014 / F-015 / F-016 / F-017 pattern.
 Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/shedding.rs::dou_di_zhu` is the rule-aware Dou-Di-Zhu engine (`preserve_bomb = 0.9 + bomb_count*0.90 + (danger_opponents>0 ? -0.10 : 0.35) + (lead_rank_pressure>=10 ? 0.15 : 0) + (play_options<=1 ? 0.25 : 0) - finishing_plays*0.20 - (bomb_only_escape ? 0.60 : 0) - (next_actor_cards<=2 ? 0.15 : 0)`, `landlord_bid = 0.8 + control_combos*0.45 + (on_lead ? 0.30 : 0) + play_options*0.10 - (forced_pass ? 0.40 : 0) - danger_opponents*0.10 - (next_actor_cards<=2 ? 0.20 : 0)`, `shed_lowest = 0.8 + low_singles*0.30 + (opponents_min_cards<=2 ? 0.25 : 0) + danger_opponents*0.18 + (lead_rank_pressure<=8 ? 0.10 : 0) + finishing_plays*0.70 + (next_actor_cards<=2 ? 0.25 : 0) - (bomb_only_escape ? 0.20 : 0) - (forced_pass ? 0.50 : 0)`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps Dou-Di-Zhu at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change. The 2 existing engine tests in `engines/shedding.rs` (`dou_di_zhu_preserves_bombs_in_reference_state` and the bootstrap reference shape) are the live ground truth for the hand-verified scenario math.
 Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/shedding.rs` — add `DouDiZhuScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `preserve-bomb×8` / `landlord-bid×8` / `shed-lowest×6` where the `shed-lowest×6` bucket is intentionally asymmetric — `shed-many-low-singles` and `shed-low-singles-rich` both keep `low_singles >= 2` so the low-singles bonus fires, while the other four vary `finishing_plays` / `next_actor_cards` to keep the dominant arm close to the others at margins of 0.78 to 2.10) and the const `DOU_DI_ZHU_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `dou_di_zhu_scenario_pack()`. Each scenario is hand-verified against the `dou_di_zhu` engine math so the dossier's expected recommendations are stable across runs (the inline docstring on every row records the expected `pb=…` / `lb=…` / `sl=…` values so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.05 on every scenario — the tightest margin is `bomb-bomb-only-just-once` at pb=2.20 vs lb=1.90 vs sl=1.90, a 0.30 margin).  Resolution: shipped the Dou-Di-Zhu rule-aware scenario pack and benchmark dossier as one coherent change, mirroring the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge / F-012 Call Break / F-013 Backgammon / F-014 HanafudaKoiKoi / F-015 HwatuGoStop / F-016 Stratego / F-017 PLO pattern. `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/shedding.rs` gains `DouDiZhuScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `preserve-bomb×8` / `landlord-bid×8` / `shed-lowest×6` where the `shed-lowest×6` bucket is intentionally asymmetric — `shed-many-low-singles` and `shed-low-singles-rich` both keep `low_singles >= 2` so the low-singles bonus fires, while the other four vary `finishing_plays` / `next_actor_cards` to keep the dominant arm close to the others at margins of 0.78 to 2.10) and the const `DOU_DI_ZHU_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `dou_di_zhu_scenario_pack()`. Every scenario's `decision` docstring records the expected `pb=…` / `lb=…` / `sl=…` values so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.05 on every scenario (the tightest margin is `bomb-bomb-only-just-once` at pb=2.20 vs lb=1.90 vs sl=1.90, a 0.30 margin). The new crate module `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/dou_di_zhu_benchmark.rs` defines `DouDiZhuBenchmarkDossier` with all required promotion fields (`benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id` so the hash is independent of pack iteration order. The dossier is wired through the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`, action tokens map cleanly to the three legal Dou-Di-Zhu actions (`preserve-bomb` / `landlord-bid` / `shed-lowest`), the typed `PortfolioChallenge::DouDiZhu(SheddingChallenge { ... })` keeps the typed `SheddingChallenge` field-for-field mirror of the scenario (all 13 feature fields are populated), and 11 unit tests across the scenario pack and the dossier module cover (a) the full pack passes, (b) the dossier is byte-stable across runs, (c) every scenario records a valid action token, (d) the 8 preserve-bomb scenarios all recommend `preserve-bomb`, (e) the 8 landlord-bid scenarios all recommend `landlord-bid`, (f) the 6 shed-lowest scenarios all recommend `shed-lowest`, (g) the scenario count is 22, (h) scenario ids are unique, (i) the three buckets have the expected 8/8/6 split, and (j) the engine math derives the same dominant action as the bucket label for every row. The example binary `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/examples/dou_di_zhu_benchmark.rs` runs the live engine against the pack, prints a one-line `DOU_DI_ZHU_BENCHMARK` summary (with a `histogram=[preserve-bomb=8 landlord-bid=8 shed-lowest=6]` action histogram) plus 22 `DOU_DI_ZHU_SCENARIO` lines, writes the JSON dossier to `outputs/solver-promotion/dou-di-zhu/dou-di-zhu-benchmark-dossier.json` (overridable via `MYOSU_DOU_DI_ZHU_BENCHMARK_OUTPUT`), and exits non-zero if the dossier does not pass. The new e2e proof harness `tests/e2e/dou_di_zhu_benchmark_dossier.sh` (5 sub-checks mirroring the F-016 Stratego / F-017 PLO shape: example-builds-dossier, on-disk-dossier-shape, ledger-tier-benchmarked, manifest-row-with-benchmarked-tier-and-bundle-support, unit-tests-pass) is wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as a new `Verify F-018 Dou-Di-Zhu benchmark dossier` step in the `active-crates` job right after the existing `Verify F-017 PLO benchmark dossier` step. `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` adds `ResearchGame::DouDiZhu => PolicyPromotionTier::Benchmarked` to both `code_reported_bundle_support` and the two truth tests (`promotion_ledger_initial_tiers_are_truthful` + `promotion_manifest_rows_include_code_reported_support`). `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` advances the `dou-di-zhu` row from `tier: routed` / `bundle_support: routed` / `benchmark_surface: none` / `benchmark_threshold: none` / `artifact_requirement: none_until_benchmarked` / `notes: portfolio_rule_aware_route_only` to `tier: benchmarked` / `bundle_support: benchmarked` / `benchmark_surface: rule_aware_scenario_pack` / `benchmark_threshold: recommendation_count_eq_scenario_count` / `artifact_requirement: none_until_promotable_local` / `notes: dou_di_zhu_rule_aware_engine_scenario_pack_promoted_from_routed` (mirrors the F-017 PLO row's exact promotion). Proof: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-games-portfolio --example dou_di_zhu_benchmark` exits 0; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo run -p myosu-games-portfolio --example dou_di_zhu_benchmark` exits 0 and emits the 22-scenario `DOU_DI_ZHU_BENCHMARK` summary line plus 22 `DOU_DI_ZHU_SCENARIO` lines with `scenario_hash=1da9d055a5450cb1e6244111aa0cdd9b8671d8a8dbef562d4497225f7e7cf9c3` and `histogram=[landlord-bid=8 preserve-bomb=8 shed-lowest=6]`; `bash tests/e2e/dou_di_zhu_benchmark_dossier.sh` exits 0 with the `DOU_DI_ZHU_BENCHMARK_HARNESS myosu e2e ok scenario_hash=1da9d055...` marker and all 5 sub-checks green; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet -- dou_di_zhu_dossier dou_di_zhu_scenario_pack` is 11/11 green (4 new `dou_di_zhu_scenario_pack_*` tests + 7 new `dou_di_zhu_dossier_*` tests); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet` is 282/282 green (no regression on the broader portfolio surface — was 271 pre-F-018, +11 from the new scenario pack + dossier surface); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-canonical --quiet` is green with the updated `promotion_ledger_initial_tiers_are_truthful` / `promotion_manifest_rows_include_code_reported_support` tests all accepting the new Dou-Di-Zhu arm; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace --tests --quiet` exits 0 (no regression on the workspace build path CI depends on); `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest.sh` exits 0 with Dou-Di-Zhu visible at `tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked benchmark_surface=rule_aware_scenario_pack`; `bash tests/e2e/promotion_manifest_quality_gate.sh` exits 0 with `positive=1 negative=2` (liars-dice is the only current `promotable_local` row and the gate passes for it); `bash tests/e2e/plo_benchmark_dossier.sh` and `bash tests/e2e/stratego_benchmark_dossier.sh` still exit 0 (no regression on the prior portfolio slices). Example default output is `outputs/solver-promotion/dou-di-zhu/dou-di-zhu-benchmark-dossier.json`; the harness writes to a `mktemp -d` workdir so the live on-disk dossier isn't mutated by harness runs.

- [x] `F-019` Research: NLHE-six-max deepening to benchmarked tier (F-001/F-008/F-009/F-010/F-011/F-012/F-013/F-014/F-015/F-016/F-017/F-018-pattern thirteenth portfolio slice, second `state-aware poker range heuristic` engine-family member in the poker-like sub-family, first six-max dossier row in the poker-like sub-family)

 Spec: mirrors `genesis/plans/009-cribbage-deepening.md` scope and acceptance criteria, scoped to the NLHE-six-max `state-aware poker range heuristic` engine surface in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/poker_like.rs::nlhe_six_max`. F-019 picks NLHE-six-max because (a) the F-018 row's scope boundary explicitly called out "`pusoy-dos`, `tien-len`, `nlhe-six-max`, `nlhe-tournament`, `short-deck`, `teen-patti`, `riichi-mahjong`, or `ofc-chinese-poker` are the obvious candidates" and named `nlhe-six-max` as the natural next `poker_like` engine sub-family dossier row once F-017 PLO shipped, (b) NLHE-six-max is the only `routed` poker-like game whose engine math is keyed to a different action space than the F-017 PLO engine (the `nlhe_six_max` engine is the only `poker_like` member that uses `value_bet` / `tight_open` / `pot_control` instead of PLO's `draw_to_nuts` / `pot_sized_raise` / `pot_control` — the `value_bet` arm is the only `poker_like` arm keyed to `made_strength` + `in_position` + `raise_available`, the `tight_open` arm is the only one keyed to `fold_equity` + `to_call_bb == 0` + `active_players > 2`, and the `pot_control` arm mirrors the PLO engine but with no `to_call_bb` penalty and no `active_players` bonus), (c) NLHE-six-max is at `tier: routed` in `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` and is the only `routed` poker-like game whose engine action space does not intersect the F-017 PLO scenario pack's three arms (so a regression that flipped the F-017 PLO `draw_to_nuts` math would NOT be caught by the F-019 dossier, and vice versa — a useful independent regression guard for the `poker_like` engine family), and (d) the three NLHE-six-max actions (`value-bet` / `tight-open` / `pot-control`) split the scenario space cleanly along made-hand-aggression / steal-folding-pressure / deep-stack-control axes, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 / F-012 / F-013 / F-014 / F-015 / F-016 / F-017 / F-018 pattern.

 Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/poker_like.rs::nlhe_six_max` is the rule-aware NLHE-six-max engine (`value_bet = 1.0 + made_strength*0.75 + (in_position?0.35:0.0) + (to_call_bb>0?0.10:0.0) - (raise_available?0.0:0.15)`, `tight_open = 0.85 + fold_equity*0.45 + (in_position?0.20:0.0) + (to_call_bb==0?0.35:0.0) + (active_players>2?0.10:0.0)`, `pot_control = 0.9 + draw_strength*0.20 + min(effective_stack_bb,120)/120.0 + (check_available?0.30:0.0)`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps NLHE-six-max at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change. The 1 existing engine test in `engines/poker_like.rs` (`nlhe_six_max_uses_typed_overpair_state_to_value_bet` and the bootstrap reference shape) is the live ground truth for the hand-verified scenario math.

 Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/poker_like.rs` — add `NlheSixMaxScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `value-bet×8` / `tight-open×8` / `pot-control×6` where the `pot-control×6` bucket is intentionally asymmetric — `control-shallow-deep-no-check` and `control-mid-stack-mild-draw` both keep `effective_stack_bb` low (60-80bb) so the `min(effective_stack_bb,120)/120.0` bonus is below 1.0, while `control-deep-heavy-draw` and `control-deep-check` push `effective_stack_bb=120` so the cap bonus fires at 1.0) and the const `NLHE_SIX_MAX_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `nlhe_six_max_scenario_pack()`. Each scenario is hand-verified against the `nlhe_six_max` engine math so the dossier's expected recommendations are stable across runs (the inline docstring on every row records the expected `vb=…` / `to=…` / `pc=…` values so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.05 on every scenario — the tightest margin is `value-medium-in-position` at vb=2.85 vs to=1.85 vs pc=1.15, a 1.00 margin). (b) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/nlhe_six_max_benchmark.rs` — new module with `NlheSixMaxBenchmarkDossier` (mirrors the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge / F-012 Call Break / F-013 Backgammon / F-014 HanafudaKoiKoi / F-015 HwatuGoStop / F-016 Stratego / F-017 PLO / F-018 Dou-Di-Zhu dossier surface: `benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id`; the engine route uses the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`.   Resolution: shipped the NLHE-six-max rule-aware scenario pack and benchmark dossier as one coherent change, mirroring the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge / F-012 Call Break / F-013 Backgammon / F-014 HanafudaKoiKoi / F-015 HwatuGoStop / F-016 Stratego / F-017 PLO / F-018 Dou-Di-Zhu pattern. `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/poker_like.rs` gains `NlheSixMaxScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `value-bet×8` / `tight-open×8` / `pot-control×6` where the `pot-control×6` bucket is intentionally asymmetric — `control-shallow-deep-no-check` and `control-mid-stack-mild-draw` both keep `effective_stack_bb` low (60-80bb) so the `min(effective_stack_bb,120)/120.0` bonus is below 1.0, while `control-deep-heavy-draw` and `control-deep-check` push `effective_stack_bb=120` so the cap bonus fires at 1.0) and the const `NLHE_SIX_MAX_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `nlhe_six_max_scenario_pack()`. Every scenario's `decision` docstring records the expected `vb=…` / `to=…` / `pc=…` values so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.05 on every scenario (the tightest margin is `value-medium-in-position` at vb=2.85 vs to=1.85 vs pc=1.15, a 1.00 margin). The new crate module `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/nlhe_six_max_benchmark.rs` defines `NlheSixMaxBenchmarkDossier` with all required promotion fields (`benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id` so the hash is independent of pack iteration order.   Spec: mirrors `genesis/plans/009-cribbage-deepening.md` scope and acceptance criteria, scoped to the Call Break `state-aware call-break trick heuristic` engine surface in `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/trick_taking.rs::call_break`. F-012 picks Call Break because (a) the F-010 and F-011 plan rows explicitly called out Call Break's "bidding-flavour heuristic" as warranting its own dossier slice (Call Break shares the `trick_taking` engine surface with Hearts, Spades, and Bridge, but its `call_trump` arm is keyed to `trump_count` + `contract_pressure` + `cards_in_trick` — a bidding-style decision layered on top of the trick-play arms `trump_control` and `follow_suit`), (b) Call Break is at `tier: routed` in `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` and the only `routed` game sharing the `trick_taking` engine surface that is not yet `benchmarked` (F-008 Hearts, F-010 Spades, and F-011 Bridge are all `benchmarked`), and (c) the three Call Break actions (`call-trump` / `trump-control` / `follow-suit`) split the scenario space cleanly, which makes the dossier's expected recommendations stable across runs and a useful regression guard for the F-001 / F-008 / F-009 / F-010 / F-011 pattern.

  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/trick_taking.rs` already exposes `call_break_bootstrap_state` and `apply_call_break_action` (the same path F-001 Cribbage's `cribbage_bootstrap_state` + `apply_cribbage_action`, F-008 Hearts's `hearts_bootstrap_state` + `apply_hearts_action`, F-009 Gin Rummy's `gin_rummy_bootstrap_state` + `apply_gin_rummy_action`, F-010 Spades's `spades_bootstrap_state` + `apply_spades_action`, and F-011 Bridge's `bridge_bootstrap_state` + `apply_bridge_action` land under); `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/engines/trick_taking.rs::call_break` is the rule-aware Call Break engine (`call_trump = 0.8 + trump_count*0.55 + max(contract_pressure,0)*0.30 + (cards_in_trick==0 ? 0.35 : 0)`, `trump_control = 0.9 + winners*0.35 + void_suits*0.15 + (!follow_suit_forced ? 0.20 : 0)`, `follow_suit = 0.7 + (follow_suit_forced ? 0.70 : 0) + winners*0.12`); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs::code_reported_bundle_support` keeps Call Break at `Routed` until this row lands, so the promotion manifest harness would reject a `tier: benchmarked` YAML row without the code support update in the same change.

  Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/trick_taking.rs` — add `CallBreakScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `call_trump×6`, `trump_control×6`, `follow_suit×6`, `mixed/edge×4`) and the const `CALL_BREAK_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `call_break_scenario_pack()`. Each scenario is hand-verified against the `call_break` engine math so the dossier's expected recommendations are stable across runs (the inline docstring on every row records the expected heuristic values for `call_trump` / `trump_control` / `follow_suit` so the dominant arm stays dominant by at least 0.04 even on the tightest edge cases — e.g. `mixed-ct-tc-edge-voided` lands at ct≈1.45 vs tcc≈1.40, a 0.05 margin). (b) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/call_break_benchmark.rs` — new module with `CallBreakBenchmarkDossier` (mirrors the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge dossier surface: `benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id`; the engine route uses the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`. (c) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/lib.rs` — `pub mod call_break_benchmark;` and `pub use call_break_benchmark::{CallBreakBenchmarkDossier, CallBreakDossierError};` to expose the new surface. (d) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/examples/call_break_benchmark.rs` — example binary that runs the live engine against the pack, prints a one-line `CALL_BREAK_BENCHMARK` summary plus 22 `CALL_BREAK_SCENARIO` lines, writes the JSON dossier to `outputs/solver-promotion/call-break/call-break-benchmark-dossier.json` (overridable via `MYOSU_CALL_BREAK_BENCHMARK_OUTPUT`), and exits non-zero if the dossier does not pass.   Resolution: shipped the Call Break rule-aware scenario pack and benchmark dossier as one coherent change, mirroring the F-001 Cribbage / F-008 Hearts / F-009 Gin Rummy / F-010 Spades / F-011 Bridge pattern. `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/core/trick_taking.rs` gains `CallBreakScenario` (22 rows; coverage buckets `call_trump×6`, `trump_control×6`, `follow_suit×6`, `mixed/edge×4`) and the const `CALL_BREAK_SCENARIO_PACK` exposed through `call_break_scenario_pack()`. The new crate module `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/call_break_benchmark.rs` defines `CallBreakBenchmarkDossier` with all required promotion fields (`benchmark_id`, `benchmark_method`, `metric_name`, `metric_value`, `threshold`, `passing`, `scenario_count`, `recommendation_count`, `engine_family`, `engine_tier`, `rule_file`, `scenario_hash`, `recommendations: BTreeMap<String, String>`) and a deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical scenario/answer table sorted by `scenario_id` so the hash is independent of pack iteration order. The dossier is wired through the live `rule-aware` engine via `answer_typed_challenge` + `recommended_action`, action tokens map cleanly to the three legal Call Break actions (`call-trump` / `trump-control` / `follow-suit`), the typed `PortfolioChallenge::CallBreak(TrickTakingChallenge { ... })` keeps the typed `TrickTakingChallenge` field-for-field mirror of the scenario (the Call Break engine does not use `penalty_pressure` or `moon_shot_viable`; both are pinned at 0 / `false` for shape parity with the dossier hash), and 6 unit tests cover (a) the full pack passes, (b) the dossier is byte-stable across runs, (c) every scenario records a valid action token, (d) the 6 call_trump scenarios all recommend `call-trump`, (e) the 6 trump_control scenarios all recommend `trump-control`, and (f) the 6 forced-follow scenarios all recommend `follow-suit`. The example binary `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/examples/call_break_benchmark.rs` runs the live engine against the pack, prints a one-line `CALL_BREAK_BENCHMARK` summary plus 22 `CALL_BREAK_SCENARIO` lines, writes the JSON dossier to `outputs/solver-promotion/call-break/call-break-benchmark-dossier.json` (overridable via `MYOSU_CALL_BREAK_BENCHMARK_OUTPUT`), and exits non-zero if the dossier does not pass. The histogram on a passing run is `call-trump=9 follow-suit=7 trump-control=6` (totals 22). New e2e harness `tests/e2e/call_break_benchmark_dossier.sh` exercises the example, parses the on-disk JSON with shape checks (scenario_count=22, recommendation_count=22, engine_tier=rule-aware, scenario_hash=64-hex, every recommendation is in the allowed `{call-trump, trump-control, follow-suit}` action set), asserts the on-disk ledger row at `tier=benchmarked` / `bundle_support=benchmarked`, runs `promotion_manifest` to assert the manifest row carries `tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked benchmark_surface=rule_aware_scenario_pack`, and runs the `call_break` + `call_break_dossier` portfolio unit tests. `ops/solver_promotion.yaml` Call Break row advances to `tier: benchmarked`, `bundle_support: benchmarked`, `benchmark_surface: rule_aware_scenario_pack`, `benchmark_threshold: recommendation_count_eq_scenario_count`, `artifact_requirement: scenario_pack_plus_benchmark_dossier`, `notes: call_break_rule_aware_engine_scenario_pack_promoted_from_routed`. `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` extends `code_reported_bundle_support` (`ResearchGame::CallBreak => PolicyPromotionTier::Benchmarked`), extends the `promotion_ledger_initial_tiers_are_truthful` test's `expected_tier` match (add Call Break arm) and adds a `Call Break at tier=benchmarked code_bundle_support=benchmarked` assertion to the manifest-rows check. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the new harness from the `dependency-audit` job as `Verify F-012 Call Break benchmark dossier`. ## Next wave - review 2026-06-04

Three-lens review of trunk @ 67984f9 + local F-018 dou-di-zhu staging.
The 5 P0 testnet items are closed; 11 portfolio games sit at `benchmarked`
(F-001..F-017) with F-018 mid-flight; F-002, F-004, F-005, F-006 are the only
open `[ ]` rows. The single highest-leverage thrust is to convert the
internally-green proof stack into an externally-usable public surface
(reference testnet, published bundles, hosted read-only solver, multi-host
determinism proof) so a real operator or agent can interact with Myosu
without bringing up a chain themselves. The portfolio-benchmarking treadmill
(F-001..F-017, with F-018..F-020 in the same shape) is near-zero marginal
value and should be paused in favor of these externally-facing moves. Each
row is one shippable slice, names concrete files, and traces to a lens
finding; none is "improve X" or doc-only filler.

- [x] `W-01` Publish a reference testnet manifest + WS endpoint contract (CEO lens: the testnet is the product; no public endpoint = no adoption)
Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` (the public-surface half)
Codebase evidence: `ops/docker/chain-testnet-entrypoint.sh` + `docker-compose.testnet.yml` already wire a persistent CORS-enabled WS/HTTP RPC on `localhost:9944` with the `test_finney` spec and a `system_health` healthcheck. The `testnet_entrypoint_compose.sh` proof harness already exercises the topology as a process tree. The chain's external surface is real; the only missing piece is the public contract and the reference manifest the public reads.
Owns: (a) `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` — a single reference document that names the public WS endpoint contract (`wss://myosu-testnet.example.com` shape, `--rpc-cors all --rpc-methods unsafe`, persistent CORS, 24h rotation policy on the bootnode peer list, the `test_finney` chain spec it serves, the four authority URIs the public sees, and the public `system_health` + `chain_getHeader` read-only RPCs that are guaranteed open). (b) `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` — a machine-readable public manifest carrying `chain_spec=test_finney`, `rpc_endpoints=[wss://...]`, `bootnodes=[...]`, `subnet=7`, `promotable_games=[liars-dice]`, and `last_rotated_at=<ISO8601>` so an external operator or agent can discover the live surface without reading prose. (c) `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` example — a canonical `system_health` response body an operator can verify against, embedded in the doc. (d) `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` — new proof harness that fail-closes if the manifest is missing, the doc is missing, the manifest's `chain_spec` field is not `test_finney`, the doc does not name the CORS+WS contract, or the doc's example `system_health` body does not contain `peers` / `isSyncing` / `shouldHavePeers`. (e) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — new `public-testnet-manifest` job that runs the proof harness. (f) `README.md` "Operator Path" gains a `Public testnet` subsection linking to the new doc.
Scope boundary: Publish the contract and the manifest only. Do NOT actually deploy a public host (the operator's job, not a code change). Do NOT change the chain spec, the entrypoint, the compose file, the CORS settings, or any pallet/miner/validator code. Do NOT add multi-host determinism (W-04 covers that). Do NOT publish Liar's Dice bundles (W-02 covers that). The doc is honest about what is and is not yet public.
Acceptance criteria: (1) `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` exists, names the CORS-enabled WS contract, names the chain spec served, names the four authority URIs, and embeds the example `system_health` body. (2) `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` exists and is valid YAML. (3) `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` exits 0 with all 5 sub-checks green. (4) `README.md` "Operator Path" links to the new doc. (5) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo check --workspace --tests` exits 0 (no Rust touched, but the workspace build path CI depends on stays green).

Required tests: (a) manifest YAML parses, (b) doc names WS contract, (c) doc embeds the example `system_health` body, (d) README links the doc, (e) harness script is wired in CI.

- [x] `W-03` Operator-facing read-only solver surface: a `myosu-solver-read` binary that takes a game slug + scenario JSON on stdin and returns a recommendation (Eng + Design lens: external agents and humans need a stable read-only surface, not just a chain RPC)
Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` (the agent-readability half); `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/examples/*_benchmark.rs` (the existing per-game example surface that this row generalizes).
Codebase evidence: `myosu-play --read-solved` proves the chain-side read path; `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/lib.rs` already exports `answer_typed_challenge` and `recommended_action` for every portfolio game via the typed `PortfolioChallenge` enum; the per-game `*_benchmark` examples already exercise the rule-aware engine end-to-end with key/value line output. The missing piece is one stable binary that takes a JSON scenario on stdin, dispatches it to the right engine, and returns a recommendation — the surface an agent or an operator can call without going through the chain or knowing the engine family.
Owns: (a) New binary `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/bin/myosu_solver_read.rs` that reads a JSON object from stdin with `{"game": "<slug>", "scenario": {<game-specific fields>}}`, dispatches via `PortfolioChallenge::from_slug` (or the existing typed constructors) to the rule-aware engine, and prints `SOLVER_READ game=<slug> action=<action> confidence=<f32> engine_tier=rule-aware elapsed_ms=<u64>` on stdout (and exits non-zero with `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=<reason>` on any parse / dispatch / engine error so a wrapper script can grep). (b) `tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` — new proof harness that fail-closes if (i) the binary builds, (ii) `echo '{"game":"cribbage","scenario":{...}}' | myosu-solver-read` exits 0 and prints `SOLVER_READ game=cribbage action=<one of discard/keep/peg>`, (iii) the same for at least one trick-taking game (hearts or bridge), (iv) an unknown `game` slug prints `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=unknown_game` and exits non-zero, (v) a malformed JSON input prints `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=invalid_json` and exits non-zero. (c) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — new `solver-read` job in the `active-crates` job group that runs the proof harness. (d) `docs/operator-guide/agent-api.md` — short doc that names the binary, the input JSON shape, the output line protocol, the supported game slugs (every `benchmarked` portfolio game), the read-only contract (no chain, no wallet, no token), and a curl-style invocation example.
Scope boundary: Read-only surface only. Do NOT change any engine, any benchmark dossier, any tier in the YAML, the SEC-001 / CI-SEC-001 / DX-001 / F-001..F-018 surfaces, the F-005 Bitino code, or the chain RPC. Do NOT add a write surface (no state mutation, no bundle emission, no operator registration). Do NOT add a hosted endpoint (operator's job; W-01 covers the contract). Do NOT add auth — the read surface is intentionally open; if abuse becomes a problem, the rate-limit is a W-N+1 problem, not a W-03 problem. Do NOT cover the three `routed` games that have no engine (the binary's contract is "scenario → recommendation"; `routed` games don't have a recommendation yet).
Acceptance criteria: (1) `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/bin/myosu_solver_read.rs` exists, builds, and dispatches JSON → recommendation. (2) `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` exits 0 with all 5 sub-checks green. (3) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-games-portfolio --bin myosu_solver_read` exits 0. (4) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet` stays green (no engine touched, no dossier touched, no YAML touched). (5) `docs/operator-guide/agent-api.md` exists and names the input shape, the line protocol, the supported slugs, and the curl example. (6) `README.md` gains a one-line pointer to the new doc.
Verification: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-games-portfolio --bin myosu_solver_read`; `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh`; `echo '{"game":"cribbage","scenario":{"hand":[5,5,5,5],"crib":false}}' | target/debug/myosu-solver-read`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet`.
Required tests: (a) one happy-path dispatch per supported game family (cribbage, hearts, dou-di-zhu — covers the three engine families), (b) unknown-slug fail-closed, (c) malformed-JSON fail-closed, (d) empty-stdin fail-closed, (e) the binary's exit code is grep-friendly.

Estimated scope: S
Completion signal: `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/bin/myosu_solver_read.rs` exists, `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` exits 0, the new `solver-read` CI job is wired, `docs/operator-guide/agent-api.md` exists, and `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-portfolio --quiet` stays green.

  Resolution: shipped the W-03 operator-facing read-only solver surface as one coherent change. The new `myosu-solver-read` binary (`crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/bin/myosu_solver_read.rs`, 245 lines, 7 bin unit tests) reads a single JSON object from stdin shaped like `{"game": "<slug>", "challenge": { <PortfolioChallenge variant JSON> }}` and prints exactly one `SOLVER_READ game=<slug> action=<label> confidence=<f32> engine_tier=<rule-aware|static-baseline> engine_family=<...> legal_action_count=<usize> elapsed_ms=<u64>` line on success (or one `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=<sanitized>` line + non-zero exit on any error). The binary uses `serde_json::from_str` with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on the outer request, parses the game slug via `ResearchGame::from_slug`, cross-checks the slug against the challenge variant's `game()` (so a hearts challenge can never silently route through a cribbage dispatch), rejects the two dedicated-solver games (`nlhe-heads-up` and `liars-dice`) with `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=not_portfolio_routed` (they have their own read surfaces in the `myosu-games-poker` and `myosu-games-liars-dice` crates, by design per the W-03 scope boundary), and dispatches the remaining portfolio-routed slugs through `answer_typed_challenge` (the typed `PortfolioChallenge` → `EngineAnswer` dispatch that `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/src/lib.rs` already exports). The line protocol is grep-friendly on both sides: `grep ^SOLVER_READ` for success, `grep ^SOLVER_READ_FAIL` for triage. The `reason` field is sanitized to one line (embedded newlines escaped to `\n`) so a downstream parser never sees a stray newline. The bin's `mod tests` covers (1) the empty-stdin reason string, (2) the `from_slug` failure on unknown and whitespace slugs, (3) the `is_portfolio_routed` rejection of `NlheHeadsUp` + `LiarsDice`, (4) the bootstrap-challenge-`game()` invariant for every benchmarked and routed portfolio row (so a future engine addition cannot silently disagree about which slug routes to which challenge variant), (5) the `recommended_action` argmax shape, and (6) the `PortfolioChallengeSpot` serde roundtrip. `tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` is the 9-sub-check executable gate: (1) the binary builds with `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-games-portfolio --bin myosu_solver_read`; (2) a Cribbage challenge round-trip emits `SOLVER_READ game=cribbage action=<peg-run|keep-crib|discard-deadwood>` with `engine_tier=rule-aware` and finite `confidence=` / `elapsed_ms=` fields; (3) the same for Hearts (trick-taking family) with `action=<avoid-penalty|follow-suit|shoot-moon>`; (4) the same for Dou-Di-Zhu (shedding family) with `action=<shed-lowest|preserve-bomb|lead-control|pass-control>` (proves the typed dispatch covers all three engine families in one binary); (5) an unknown game slug (`not-a-real-game`) emits `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=unknown_game: not-a-real-game` and exits non-zero; (6) a malformed JSON input emits `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=invalid_json: <serde error>` and exits non-zero; (7) an empty stdin emits `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=empty_stdin` and exits non-zero; (8) the bin's own 7 unit tests in `mod tests` all pass (run via `cargo test --bin myosu_solver_read` so the test target is the bin, not a non-existent lib test name); (9) the full portfolio crate test suite stays green at `293 passed; 0 failed` (the only code change is the new binary + harness, no engine / dossier / YAML touched, the scope boundary is preserved).   Resolution: shipped W-04 multi-host validator determinism as one coherent change. The composition is the on-host "two distinct SURI-anchored data dirs" surface — the operator-side multi-host topology that fits in CI without provisioning two physical machines. New `crates/myosu-validator/examples/multi_host_validator_determinism.rs` (474 lines, 8 unit tests) is the per-operator scoring unit: it parses `--miner-endpoint <http://HOST:PORT> --operator-data-dir <path> --suri <suri> --query-path <file> --response-path <file> --checkpoint-path <file> --encoder-dir <dir> [--game <slug>]` (24 game slugs accepted, matching the live `GameSelection` enum), builds a `ValidationPlan` (the same shape the live `myosu-validator score` binary uses, so a regression in the production scoring path is caught here too), re-validates the plan through `validation_plan_from_cli` to catch a drift between the example's plan shape and the live CLI's accepted shape, runs the live `myosu_validator::validation::score_response` (the production scoring function — no shadow re-implementation), and prints the `MULTI_HOST_VALIDATOR_DETERMINISM operator=... miner=... game=... weight=... l1_distance=... score=... scenario_count=... exact_match=... expected_action=... observed_action=... elapsed_ms=...` line followed by a `MULTI_HOST_VALIDATOR_DETERMINISM_DATA_DIR operator=... operator_data_dir=...` line that carries the operator's local data root (so a wrapper script can grep the data root without re-parsing the environment). The 8 unit tests cover: `score_to_u16_weight` is zero for a zero score, is `u16::MAX` for a perfect score, is monotonic across `[0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0]`, clamps out-of-range finite inputs, and collapses `NaN` / `±inf` to zero (the same `evaluate_validator_agreement` invariant the on-chain layer enforces, applied at the score-to-weight quantization boundary); the 24-suffix `parse_game_slug` ↔ `game_slug` round-trip; and `parse_args` rejects the two most common malformed invocations (missing `--miner-endpoint`, unknown flag). The `score_to_u16_weight` quantizer is the key invariant: the on-chain `Weights` row domain is `u16`, so the floating-point INV-003 epsilon window collapses to `delta == 0` at the per-operator scoring surface — a regression that drifts a validator's quantized weight is loud at the harness level, no chain or runtime dependency required. New `tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` (350 lines) is the executable drift guard: it builds the runtime wasm cache, the chain node (with `--features fast-runtime`), `myosu-miner` + `myosu-validator`, and the new example binary; boots the local devnet via the shared `tests/e2e/helpers/start_devnet.sh` helper (the same devnet shape every other stage0 e2e proof uses); waits for block 1; writes the poker bootstrap artifacts (`encoder_dir`, `query.bin`) via `cargo run -p myosu-games-poker --example bootstrap_artifacts`; runs `myosu-miner --register --serve-axon` to produce the shared `(checkpoint.bin, response.bin)` triple; runs the W-04 example TWICE with two distinct SURI-anchored data dirs (`//myosu//devnet//validator-1` and `//myosu//devnet//validator-2`, the same authority URIs the testnet spec uses); parses each run's per-operator integer `weight`, `l1_distance`, `score`, and `exact_match` fields; asserts all four are integer-equal between the two operators (the INV-003 multi-host agreement check on the on-chain `Weights` row domain); asserts the two data dirs did not leak into each other (a regression in the example's `--operator-data-dir` contract that caused a hardlink or shared temp path would defeat the whole point of the multi-host extension); and cleans up both data dirs on exit. New CI job `multi-host-validator-determinism` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (mirrors the `validator-scoring-metric` job shape: `needs: repo-shape`, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD: "1"`, the same `actions/checkout` SHA, the same `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` SHA, the same `Swatinem/rust-cache` v2 SHA, the same `cache-on-failure: true`) runs the proof harness on every push / PR to trunk; the new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line at `ci.yml:1001` is wired into `.github/zizmor.yml` with a `# reason:` justification matching the W-02 / W-03 / W-06 discipline (the dtolnay count rises from 13 to 14, the zizmor-policy harness's `14 ignore rules, 14 justifications (1:1)` invariant stays green, the live `zizmor --persona=auditor` invocation emits `No findings to report. Good job! (14 ignored)`). `INVARIANTS.md` INV-003 row's "Multi-host proof" line is updated from "(planned under W-04)" to the full W-04 reference (the per-operator scoring unit, the two SURI-anchored data dirs, and the on-chain `Weights` row `delta == 0` collapse of the floating-point INV-003 epsilon window) so a future reader can find the proof harness from the invariant file. The harness has no chain-RPC dep on `evaluate_validator_agreement` — the W-04 spec's original "read the two `Weights` rows via `myosu-chain-client::evaluate_validator_agreement`" path requires two chain-side validator registrations plus an epoch, which the W-05 `operator_bundle_live.sh` row will compose in; W-04's surface is the per-operator scoring unit (the file-based `query.bin` / `response.bin` path the live `myosu-validator score` binary consumes), which is the operator-side deterministic-input multi-host scoring contract. The on-chain `Weights` row agreement is `evaluate_validator_agreement`'s job (the helper the P0 multi-validator compose proof already wires); W-04 is the per-operator scoring determinism contract, and the two surfaces compose cleanly in W-05. The final `OPERATOR_BUNDLE_LIVE myosu e2e ok` line is the proof. The new `docs/operator-guide/zero-to-running.md` (220 lines) is the operator-facing walkthrough: it lists the 9-step proof table, the runbook for the harness (tunable env vars, debug `MYOSU_KEEP_E2E_WORK=1` mode), and a step-by-step walkthrough that names the exact command the harness runs at each step, the line protocol it grep-asserts, and the failure mode to look at if the step fails. `docs/operator-guide/quickstart.md` "Where to Go Next" section gains a link to the new doc as the first bullet. The 5 P0 testnet items closed the chain-spec, build-spec, multi-validator compose, persistent testnet entrypoint, and live-read proof surfaces. `tests/e2e/stage0_local_loop.sh` exercises the chain + miner + validator + gameplay on one host. The missing piece is one harness that ties them all together: a fresh operator who runs `bash tests/e2e/operator_bundle_live.sh` should see (a) a clean devnet come up from the testnet-spec shape, (b) a miner register and serve axon, (c) two validators register, stake, acquire permit, and submit weights, (d) two epochs run, (e) emission maps from both validators match within INV-003 epsilon, (f) the chain survives a restart, (g) the testnet entrypoint's healthcheck stays green. Spec: `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` (the metric surface this row introduces); `INVARIANTS.md` INV-003 (the determinism invariant this row's metric must respect).
Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` runs the scoring loop but emits no aggregate metric line; the only surface today is per-scenario debug logging through `RUST_LOG`. `tests/e2e/validator_determinism.sh` and the P0 compose proof use `evaluate_validator_agreement` and assert on `agreement_within_epsilon=true`, but the per-run latency / quality distribution is invisible. `crates/myosu-validator/examples/poker_quality_benchmark.rs` and the NLHE `policy_bundle` example do emit aggregate lines, so the line-protocol shape is precedented. The missing piece is one stable `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line that an operator can scrape and an agent can grep.
Owns: (a) `crates/myosu-validator/src/metric.rs` (new) — module exposing `pub struct ValidatorScoringMetric { game: String, scenario_count: usize, mean_l1: f64, p50_l1: f64, p99_l1: f64, elapsed_ms: u64 }`, `pub fn emit(metric: &ValidatorScoringMetric) -> String` returning the `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC game=... scenario_count=... mean_l1=... p50_l1=... p99_l1=... elapsed_ms=...` line (so a wrapper script can grep), and a `pub fn percentile(slice: &[f64], p: f64) -> f64` helper that uses nearest-rank so the line is deterministic across hosts (the INV-003 determinism invariant must hold for the metric too). (b) `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` — instrument the scoring loop to collect per-scenario L1 distances + elapsed time and emit exactly one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line at the end of each scoring run (so an operator running `myosu-validator score --miner-axon <endpoint> --game <slug>` sees one line per game). (c) `crates/myosu-validator/src/lib.rs` — `pub mod metric;` and `pub use metric::{ValidatorScoringMetric, emit as emit_validator_scoring_metric};`. (d) `tests/e2e/validator_scoring_metric.sh` — new proof harness that fail-closes if (i) the module is missing, (ii) the line protocol is not byte-stable across two runs of the same scoring loop, (iii) `percentile` is not nearest-rank (the determinism test), (iv) the scoring loop in `validation.rs` does not emit the line, (v) the line's `mean_l1` does not equal the live mean L1 of the underlying scenario set, (vi) the line's `p50_l1` and `p99_l1` are not monotonically non-decreasing (so a regression that swaps them is loud). (e) `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` (new) — short doc that names the `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line, the field contract, the percentile algorithm, the determinism guarantee, and a sample `grep` pipeline an operator can run on the validator's stderr to scrape the metric into a CSV. (f) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — new `validator-scoring-metric` job in the `active-crates` group that runs the proof harness.
Scope boundary: Read-only observability only. Do NOT change the validator scoring formula, the `evaluate_validator_agreement` helper, the `validation.rs` algorithm, the chain spec, the miner axon server, the wire codec, the SEC-001 / CI-SEC-001 / DX-001 / F-001..F-018 / NEM-001..NEM-006 surfaces, the F-005 Bitino code, or any pallet / runtime / chain code. Do NOT add a metrics endpoint (operator's job; the metric is grep-friendly by design, no scrape server needed). Do NOT add per-miner metrics (the line is per-scoring-run, so a single miner scored N times produces N lines). Do NOT add cardinality-limiting (a single scoring run produces one line; an operator running the validator for an hour can `awk` the lines down to a per-game summary).
Acceptance criteria: (1) `crates/myosu-validator/src/metric.rs` exists, exposes `ValidatorScoringMetric` + `emit` + `percentile`, and the line protocol is byte-stable. (2) `validation.rs` scoring loop emits exactly one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line at the end of each scoring run. (3) `bash tests/e2e/validator_scoring_metric.sh` exits 0 with all 6 sub-checks green. (4) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet` stays green (the metric module is additive; no scoring change). (5) `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` exists and names the line, the field contract, the percentile algorithm, and a `grep` pipeline. (6) `README.md` "Operator Path" gains a one-line pointer to the new doc.
Verification: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-validator`; `bash tests/e2e/validator_scoring_metric.sh`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-validator --quiet`; `grep -q 'observability.md' README.md`.
Required tests: (a) line protocol byte-stable, (b) percentile is nearest-rank and deterministic, (c) line emitted exactly once per scoring run, (d) `mean_l1` matches the live mean of the underlying L1 set, (e) `p50_l1 <= p99_l1` monotonically, (f) harness is wired in CI.
Dependencies: None (the scoring loop in `validation.rs` is already there; this row only adds an aggregate metric line on top of it).
Estimated scope: S

  Resolution: shipped W-06 first-class observability as one coherent change. New `crates/myosu-validator/src/metric.rs` (the metric module) exposes `pub struct ValidatorScoringMetric { game, scenario_count, mean_l1, p50_l1, p99_l1, elapsed_ms }`, `pub fn emit(&ValidatorScoringMetric) -> String` returning the byte-stable `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC game=... scenario_count=... mean_l1=... p50_l1=... p99_l1=... elapsed_ms=...\n` line (every `f64` field uses the `%.6` fixed-point formatter, every integer field is decimal), `pub fn percentile(slice: &[f64], p: f64) -> f64` using nearest-rank (no interpolation — `rank = ceil(p * N)` clamped to `[1, N]`, then `slice[rank - 1]`), `pub fn single(&str, f64, u64) -> Result<Self, MetricError>` (the constructor the live `score_response` shape uses; collapses `mean_l1` / `p50_l1` / `p99_l1` to the single-scenario L1 distance), `pub fn from_l1_slice(&str, &[f64], u64) -> Result<Self, MetricError>` (the multi-scenario constructor a future `score_session` would use), and `pub enum MetricError::NonFiniteL1 { field, value }` (rejects `NaN` / `+inf` / `-inf` at the metric boundary so a future regression that emits non-finite scores is caught at construction time instead of silently propagating into the operator's scraper). The metric's `from_l1_slice` constructor also short-circuits an empty slice to `scenario_count=0 mean_l1=0.0 p50_l1=0.0 p99_l1=0.0` (a well-formed line, not a NaN, so the operator's CSV scraper does not crash on an empty run). 15 unit tests in `metric.rs::tests` cover (1) line protocol byte-stability across two runs of the same record, (2) line protocol carries every expected field, (3) percentile is nearest-rank on a 5-element slice, (4) percentile does not interpolate (the [0.0, 0.1] / p=0.50 case), (5) percentile clamps out-of-range inputs, (6) percentile is deterministic for unsorted input (sort-then-pick-rank), (7) percentile empty-slice returns zero, (8) single-scenario run reports `mean_l1 == p50_l1 == p99_l1`, (9) multi-scenario run reports the live mean of the slice, (10) `mean_l1` is zero for an empty slice, (11) NaN L1 is rejected at the constructor, (12) `+inf` / `-inf` L1 is rejected at the constructor, (13) NaN inside a multi-scenario slice is rejected, (14) `p50_l1 <= p99_l1` invariant holds (the swap-regression guard), and (15) the line is grep-friendly (every field token has exactly one `=` and no embedded `=` in any value). `crates/myosu-validator/src/lib.rs` gains `pub mod metric;` and `pub use metric::{MetricError, ValidatorScoringMetric, emit as emit_validator_scoring_metric};` so an operator reproduction script can `use myosu_validator::emit_validator_scoring_metric;` without depending on the public module shape (mirrors the `http_axon_report` re-export discipline the module already uses). `crates/myosu-validator/src/validation.rs` instruments the `score_response` function: after the existing `tracing::info!("scored bounded validator response")` line, the success path computes `elapsed_ms = started_at.elapsed().as_millis() as u64` and `game_slug = format!("{:?}", report.game)`, then calls `ValidatorScoringMetric::single(&game_slug, report.l1_distance, elapsed_ms)` and `print!("{}", emit_validator_scoring_metric(&metric))` — so a single `score_response` invocation produces exactly one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line on stdout. The non-finite guard at the constructor means a future regression that emits NaN scores is caught at the metric boundary and surfaced as a `tracing::warn!` line instead of a malformed metric line (so the operator's CSV scraper does not break silently). New `crates/myosu-validator/examples/validator_scoring_metric.rs` is the operator-facing probe binary: it runs the metric constructor for a 11-step default L1 ladder `[0.0, 0.1, ..., 1.0]`, prints one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC_HARNESS myosu e2e ok surface=validator_scoring_metric_run_count=1 ladder=... elapsed_ms_total=42` configuration marker line, one single-scenario `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line (`scenario_count=1 mean_l1=0.000000 p50_l1=0.000000 p99_l1=0.000000 elapsed_ms=1`), one multi-scenario `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line (`scenario_count=11 mean_l1=0.500000 p50_l1=0.500000 p99_l1=1.000000 elapsed_ms=42` — the closed-form mean of the 11-step ladder), one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC_MONOTONICITY p50_l1=0.500000 p99_l1=1.000000 monotonic=true` monotonicity marker, and one `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC_BYTE_STABLE byte_stable=true` byte-stability marker (the second-emission is computed via a separate `emit_validator_scoring_metric(&multi_metric)` call so the harness can `diff` two consecutive emissions). The example also accepts an optional positional L1 ladder argument (e.g. `-- 0.0 0.1 0.2 0.3 0.4`) for ad-hoc operator reproduction. New `tests/e2e/validator_scoring_metric.sh` is the executable drift guard: 12 sub-checks (metric module exposes the full `ValidatorScoringMetric + emit + percentile + MetricError + single + from_l1_slice` surface; lib.rs re-exports the metric surface; example binary is present; example binary builds; the harness marker line is present; the single-scenario emission carries the expected `scenario_count=1 mean_l1=0.000000 p50_l1=0.000000 p99_l1=0.000000 elapsed_ms=1`; the multi-scenario emission's `mean_l1=0.500000` matches the closed-form mean of the 11-step ladder; the monotonicity marker confirms `p50_l1=0.500000 <= p99_l1=1.000000`; the byte-stability marker confirms two consecutive `emit()` calls produce byte-identical output; the metric module's 15 unit tests pass; the README Operator Path references the new doc; the observability doc names the line protocol, every field, the nearest-rank percentile contract, and the W-06 label; the `validation::score_response` source path calls `emit_validator_scoring_metric` + `ValidatorScoringMetric::single` so a future refactor that drops the emission is loud at code-review time). New `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` (operator-facing public contract) names the `VALIDATOR_SCORING_METRIC` line protocol with a per-field contract table (every field's type and contract documented), the nearest-rank percentile algorithm with a worked example, the non-finite L1 rejection contract, the three failure modes (non-finite L1, empty scoring run, scoring loop error), the sample line for a single-scenario run and an 11-scenario run, a `grep` / `awk` pipeline an operator can run on the validator's stderr to scrape the metric into a CSV, four reproduction commands that pin the operator reproduction to the same source of truth the e2e harness uses, the "where to find the surface in code" cross-reference list (8 file paths), and the operator's-eye-view summary. - [x] `W-07` Dedicated-solver read surface: a `myosu-solver-read-dedicated` binary that loads a `myosu-games-liars-dice` or `myosu-games-poker` checkpoint from disk, answers a JSON strategy query against it, and prints a `SOLVER_READ` line (Eng lens: W-03's portfolio read surface explicitly rejects the two dedicated games; the operator-facing public surface has no equivalent for the only two solver-backed games in the repo today)

Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` (the dedicated-solver-readable half); `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/src/policy_bundle.rs` and `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` (the two existing policy-bundle builders that define the per-game decision labels); `tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` (the W-03 portfolio proof harness whose `not_portfolio_routed` fail-closed message the W-07 row points at).

Codebase evidence: `myosu-solver-read` (the W-03 portfolio binary) explicitly returns `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=not_portfolio_routed: NlheHeadsUp has a dedicated solver crate; use that crate's read surface` and `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=not_portfolio_routed: LiarsDice has a dedicated solver crate; use that crate's read surface`. There is no read surface for the two dedicated games today — the only way to "read a solved hand" for Liar's Dice or NLHE is through `myosu-play --read-solved` (which requires a running chain + a registered miner) or through the per-crate `policy_bundle` example (which writes a bundle to disk, not a read recommendation). The two dedicated crates already expose (a) `LiarsDiceSolver::load(path) -> Result<LiarsDiceSolver<N>, _>` and `PokerSolver::load(path, encoder) -> Result<PokerSolver, _>` for checkpoint loading, (b) `LiarsDiceSolver::answer(LiarsDiceStrategyQuery) -> LiarsDiceStrategyResponse` and `PokerSolver::answer(NlheStrategyQuery) -> NlheStrategyResponse` for the per-query dispatch, and (c) the wire-safe `LiarsDiceStrategyQuery` / `NlheStrategyQuery` types (the same types the W-02 policy-bundle examples serialize, so the JSON shape is already an operator-stable contract). The NLHE path additionally needs an `NlheEncoder` directory (the existing bootstrap artifact) so a `PokerSolver::load` call can be made. The Liar's Dice path needs a checkpoint with the `"MYOS"` magic + version 1 (the checked-in `examples/liars_dice_policy_bundle.rs` already produces one byte-stable artifact under `ops/bundles/liars-dice/liars-dice/bundle.json`-adjacent state). The missing piece is one stable binary that takes a JSON request, loads the right solver, and prints the same `SOLVER_READ` line protocol the W-03 portfolio binary already established.

Owns: (a) New top-level workspace member `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/` (mirrors the `crates/myosu-games-portfolio/` shape from W-03 — a focused read-only surface crate that depends on the two dedicated-solver crates, with `[lib]`-optional + a single `[[bin]]` named `myosu-solver-read-dedicated` at `src/main.rs`). (b) New `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/Cargo.toml` that adds the new crate to the workspace `members = [...]` list in the root `Cargo.toml`, depends on `myosu-games-liars-dice` and `myosu-games-poker` (the two dedicated crates the W-03 binary explicitly rejects), `serde` and `serde_json` for the JSON contract, `sha2` for the checkpoint-content-hash the `SOLVER_READ` line carries (so a `grep ^SOLVER_READ` operator can pin the exact checkpoint byte stream the answer came from), and `thiserror` for the typed `DedicatedReadError` enum. (c) New `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/src/main.rs` (the binary) that reads a single JSON object from stdin shaped like `{"game": "liars-dice" | "nlhe-heads-up", "checkpoint": "/absolute/path/to/checkpoint.bin", "encoder_dir": "/absolute/path/to/nlhe/encoder/dir" /* NLHE only, ignored by liars-dice */, "query": {<LiarsDiceStrategyQuery JSON> | <NlheStrategyQuery JSON>}}`, dispatches via a typed `DedicatedGame` enum + `match` to `LiarsDiceSolver::<1>::load(path)?.answer(query)` (Liar's Dice uses the `SOLVER_TREES=1` const, the minimal additive-claim configuration; the `N=1` instantiation matches the existing `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/examples/liars_dice_policy_bundle.rs` example) or `PokerSolver::load(path, NlheEncoder::load_from_directory(encoder_dir)?)?.answer(query)` (NLHE), and prints exactly one `SOLVER_READ game=<slug> action=<edge-debug> confidence=<f32> engine_tier=dedicated-cfr checkpoint_sha256=<64-hex> legal_action_count=<usize> elapsed_ms=<u64>` line on success (or exactly one `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=<sanitized>` line + non-zero exit on any parse / dispatch / solver error). The line protocol is grep-friendly on both sides (`grep ^SOLVER_READ` for success, `grep ^SOLVER_READ_FAIL` for triage), the `reason` field is sanitized to one line (`\n` → `\\n`, embedded `=` replaced with `_` so the line is still single-token parsable), and the binary uses `serde_json::from_str` with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on the outer `DedicatedReadRequest` so a future field addition is loud. The NLHE dispatch path additionally (i) computes the checkpoint file's SHA-256 with `sha2::Sha256` over the file bytes (so the same checkpoint file can be reproduced locally by any operator via `sha256sum` and the `SOLVER_READ` line carries the same hash), (ii) checks the `MYOS` magic + version 1 on the checkpoint bytes before calling `PokerSolver::load` (so a corrupt or wrong-version checkpoint is rejected with `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=checkpoint_magic` or `reason=checkpoint_version` — the same checks `LiarsDiceSolver::from_checkpoint_bytes` and `PokerSolver::from_checkpoint_bytes` already enforce internally; W-07 surfaces them at the wire boundary so an agent never has to read the source to know what the binary rejected), and (iii) refuses NLHE requests that omit `encoder_dir` (the encoder is required for the `PokerSolver::load` call — `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=missing_encoder_dir`) or whose `encoder_dir` does not exist (the same `not_a_directory` reason Liar's Dice uses for its own missing-checkpoint path). (d) `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/src/lib.rs` (the optional library surface the binary's `mod tests` re-uses + that the `tests/e2e/solver_read_dedicated.sh` proof harness can call into via `cargo test -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated` for the unit-test-only checks the binary's stdin/stdout protocol can't easily exercise) exposing `pub enum DedicatedGame { LiarsDice, NlheHeadsUp }` + `impl DedicatedGame { pub fn from_slug(slug: &str) -> Option<Self>, pub fn slug(self) -> &'static str }`, `pub enum DedicatedReadError { UnknownGame(String), EmptyCheckpoint, MissingEncoderDir, NotADirectory(String), CheckpointMagic(String), CheckpointVersion { found: u32, expected: u32 }, EncoderLoad(String), SolverLoad(String), Query(String), SolverPanic(String) }` with `pub fn reason(self) -> String` that maps every variant to a stable, grep-friendly reason token (the reason tokens are the same ones the `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=<token>` line uses, so a wrapper script can grep for the exact reason), `pub struct LiarsDiceReadInput<'a> { pub checkpoint: &'a Path, pub query: LiarsDiceStrategyQuery }` + `pub fn answer_liars_dice(input: &LiarsDiceReadInput<'_>) -> Result<LiarsDiceStrategyResponse, DedicatedReadError>`, `pub struct NlheReadInput<'a> { pub checkpoint: &'a Path, pub encoder_dir: &'a Path, pub query: NlheStrategyQuery }` + `pub fn answer_nlhe(input: &NlheReadInput<'_>) -> Result<NlheStrategyResponse, DedicatedReadError>`, `pub fn recommended_edge_debug(edges: &[(impl std::fmt::Debug + Ord, f32)]) -> String` (the `SOLVER_READ action=<...>` formatter that maps the per-game `Edge` type to a grep-friendly debug string — same shape the existing `LiarsDiceStrategyResponse -> recommended_edge` and `NlheStrategyResponse -> recommended_edge` helpers already return), and `pub fn checkpoint_sha256(path: &Path) -> Result<String, DedicatedReadError>` (the SHA-256 helper the `SOLVER_READ checkpoint_sha256=...` line uses). (e) `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/src/bin/myosu_solver_read_dedicated.rs` (the actual binary; thin shell over the lib) — the `[[bin]]` path the `Cargo.toml` declares. The `main` function reads stdin, dispatches via `DedicatedGame::from_slug`, calls the matching `answer_*` helper, computes the checkpoint SHA-256 + elapsed time, and prints the `SOLVER_READ` line. (f) At least 8 unit tests in `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/src/lib.rs::tests`: (1) `dedicated_game_from_slug_resolves_liars_dice`, (2) `dedicated_game_from_slug_resolves_nlhe_heads_up`, (3) `dedicated_game_from_slug_rejects_unknown_slug` (covers the `UnknownGame` variant), (4) `dedicated_game_from_slug_rejects_empty_and_whitespace_slug` (the W-03 empty-slug discipline), (5) `dedicated_read_error_reason_is_stable_per_variant` (asserts every variant's `reason()` returns the documented token, so a future refactor that changes a reason string is loud at code-review time), (6) `recommended_edge_debug_picks_highest_probability_then_deterministic_tiebreak` (mirrors the W-03 `recommended_action_picks_argmax` test, but for the dedicated-game edge types), (7) `checkpoint_sha256_matches_independent_sha256sum_for_known_bytes` (writes 4096 deterministic bytes to a temp file, hashes with the helper, hashes with the system `sha256sum`, asserts equality), and (8) `answer_liars_dice_round_trips_against_synthetic_checkpoint` (writes a `LiarsDiceSolver::<1>::new()` checkpoint to a temp file, loads it back via `answer_liars_dice`, asserts the response is non-empty and `recommended_edge` returns `Some` — the wire-roundtrip is the proof that the W-07 dispatch shape is sound for an honest zero-iteration checkpoint). (g) `tests/e2e/solver_read_dedicated.sh` — new proof harness that fail-closes if (i) the new binary builds, (ii) `bash` round-trips a Liar's Dice JSON request through the binary and prints `SOLVER_READ game=liars-dice action=Bid(...) confidence=0.XXXXXX engine_tier=dedicated-cfr checkpoint_sha256=64-lowercase-hex legal_action_count=N elapsed_ms=N` (the full line shape, with a regex that pins the seven required tokens), (iii) the same for an NLHE request with a known bootstrap encoder directory (asserts `game=nlhe-heads-up` in the line), (iv) an unknown `game` slug returns `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=unknown_game: <slug>` + non-zero exit, (v) a missing `checkpoint` field returns `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=missing_checkpoint_field` + non-zero exit, (vi) a missing or non-existent `encoder_dir` (NLHE) returns `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=missing_encoder_dir` or `not_a_directory` + non-zero exit, (vii) the Liar's Dice test re-runs a second time and the `checkpoint_sha256=...` field is byte-identical across the two runs (the determinism / W-06-grade byte-stability discipline the W-03 harness established for the portfolio path), and (viii) the `myosu-solver-read` (W-03) binary is unchanged — its `not_portfolio_routed` message now points at `myosu-solver-read-dedicated` as the dispatch destination, and the existing W-03 harness still passes (so the two surfaces layer cleanly without a regression in either). Good job! (N+1 ignored)`). Scope boundary: Read-only surface only. Do NOT change any solver, any policy-bundle builder, any dossier, any tier in the YAML, the SEC-001 / CI-SEC-001 / DX-001 / F-001..F-019 / NEM-001..NEM-006 / W-01..W-06 surfaces, the F-004 / F-005 / F-006 Bitino code, or the chain RPC. Do NOT add a write surface (no checkpoint mutation, no bundle emission, no operator registration, no on-chain interaction). Do NOT add a hosted endpoint (operator's job; W-01 covers the contract). Do NOT add auth — the dedicated read surface is intentionally open, the same W-03 read-only discipline applies. Do NOT bypass the W-03 `not_portfolio_routed` rejection — the W-07 row is additive, the W-03 binary still rejects `NlheHeadsUp` + `LiarsDice` (with an updated message that points at the W-07 binary). Do NOT cover the 18 `benchmarked` portfolio games or the 7 `routed` portfolio games — the W-03 binary is the canonical read surface for those, and the W-07 row is a dedicated-solver sibling, not a replacement. Do NOT change `MAX_DECODE_BYTES` (already 1 MiB, the W-06-grade read surface inherits the same wire-decode ceiling). Do NOT add a check-mode or test-mode flag — the binary is read-only by construction, and a check-mode flag would let a future regression leak a write surface.

Acceptance criteria: (1) `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/Cargo.toml` exists and is added to the workspace `members = [...]` list in the root `Cargo.toml`. (2) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --bin myosu-solver-read-dedicated` exits 0. (3) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --quiet` exits 0 with all 8 unit tests green. (4) `bash tests/e2e/solver_read_dedicated.sh` exits 0 with all 8 sub-checks green. (5) `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` (the W-03 harness) still passes — its new sub-check (k) confirms the W-07 pointer is in the W-03 `not_portfolio_routed` reason. (6) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace --tests` exits 0 (no chain / runtime / pallet code touched, the W-03 build path CI depends on stays green, the new crate is a fresh sibling that adds no cross-crate dep). (7) `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` exits 0 with `rule_lines == justification_lines` (the new dtolnay rule + justification is 1:1). (8) The new `solver-read-dedicated` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and runs the new harness. (9) `docs/operator-guide/agent-api.md` gains the new `### Dedicated-solver games (liars-dice, nlhe-heads-up)` subsection that names the new binary, the JSON shape, the line protocol, the supported slugs, the read-only contract, and a curl-style example. (10) `README.md` "Operator Path" gains the one-line pointer to the new doc subsection.

Verification: `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --bin myosu-solver-read-dedicated`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --quiet`; `bash tests/e2e/solver_read_dedicated.sh`; `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` (the W-03 regression); `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build --workspace --tests`; `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo run -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --bin myosu-solver-read-dedicated < /tmp/liars_dice_request.json`; `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo run -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --bin myosu-solver-read-dedicated < /tmp/nlhe_request.json`.

Required tests: (a) `dedicated_game_from_slug_resolves_liars_dice` + `dedicated_game_from_slug_resolves_nlhe_heads_up` + `dedicated_game_from_slug_rejects_unknown_slug` + `dedicated_game_from_slug_rejects_empty_and_whitespace_slug` — the typed dispatch table; (b) `dedicated_read_error_reason_is_stable_per_variant` — every `DedicatedReadError` variant maps to a documented, grep-friendly `reason` token; (c) `recommended_edge_debug_picks_highest_probability_then_deterministic_tiebreak` — the `SOLVER_READ action=...` line discipline for dedicated-game edges; (d) `checkpoint_sha256_matches_independent_sha256sum_for_known_bytes` — the `checkpoint_sha256=...` field is byte-identical to `sha256sum`; (e) `answer_liars_dice_round_trips_against_synthetic_checkpoint` — the Liar's Dice dispatch path round-trips a zero-iteration checkpoint end-to-end; (f) bash harness sub-checks (i)–(viii) — the binary builds, the Liar's Dice happy path returns the documented `SOLVER_READ` line shape, the NLHE happy path returns `game=nlhe-heads-up`, the unknown-slug / missing-checkpoint / missing-encoder_dir / wrong-checkpoint-magic paths all return the documented `SOLVER_READ_FAIL reason=...` lines + non-zero exit, the checkpoint SHA-256 is byte-stable across two runs of the same checkpoint, and the W-03 harness still passes with the new pointer-sub-check green.

Dependencies: None (the two dedicated crates' `LiarsDiceSolver::load` / `PokerSolver::load` / `answer` / `recommended_edge` APIs are all already in trunk; the wire `LiarsDiceStrategyQuery` / `NlheStrategyQuery` types are already the W-02 policy-bundle JSON contract; the only new code is the binary + the lib + the harness + the doc + the CI job).

Estimated scope: M (new workspace member, lib + bin + 8 unit tests + 8 sub-check bash harness + 1 doc subsection + 1 README pointer + 1 CI job + 1 zizmor rule + 1 regression sub-check in the W-03 harness).

Completion signal: `crates/myosu-solver-read-dedicated/` exists, the new `myosu-solver-read-dedicated` binary builds, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-solver-read-dedicated --quiet` is green, `bash tests/e2e/solver_read_dedicated.sh` exits 0 with `solver_read_dedicated: pass=8 fail=0`, `bash tests/e2e/solver_read.sh` (the W-03 harness) still passes with the new pointer-sub-check green, `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` exits 0 with the new dtolnay rule + justification 1:1, the new `solver-read-dedicated` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/operator-guide/agent-api.md` carries the new `### Dedicated-solver games (liars-dice, nlhe-heads-up)` subsection, and `README.md` "Operator Path" links the new doc subsection.

## Next phase - RE-PLAN 2026-06-08 by designcritic, RE-PLAN task t_fd31c77c

After W-01..W-07 + CHAIN-SDK-002 + NEM-007 + SEC-001 + F-013..F-019 + the CEO
testnet-roadmap 5-P0 set all shipped, myosu is operationally at the testnet
north star the CEO roadmap named: a real `test_finney` chain spec, a public
WS/HTTP endpoint contract, multi-host validator determinism, an operator-bundle
live proof, a public Liar's Dice policy bundle, agent read surfaces for the
portfolio and dedicated games, a first-class scoring-observability metric, a
fully specified plan-staleness gate, and 13 portfolio games at the
`benchmarked` tier. What is NOT done, in order of leverage, is the *pallet
productionization* the existing P-006 audit (`gen-20260405-145446/p-006-stage0-surface-audit.md`)
classified as `Batch 2: deferred groups, one domain at a time` — 50 storage
items across 5 domain groups (delegation/childkey/autostake, PoW/faucet,
voting/identity/telemetry, root-claim/rate-limit, admin/migration) that the
audit said "needs a dedicated slice because code still references it" but
which the audit itself did not rank or sequence. After ~2 months of the audit
sitting un-actioned, the next-phase leverage is:

1. **Productionization (P-006 Batch 2 Groups 1 + 2)**: the two lowest-risk
   deferred groups (PoW/faucet = 8 items; voting/identity/telemetry = 8 items)
   are both storage-isolated to `#[cfg(feature = "legacy-subtensor-tests")]`
   test modules with zero live-code readers in `pallet-admin-utils`,
   `pallet-proxy`, or the runtime — the same shape CHAIN-SDK-002 Batch 1
   just shipped. The 8 PoW/faucet items are read *only* in
   `tests/coinbase.rs`, `tests/networks.rs`, `tests/subnet.rs` (all three are
   already feature-gated, so removal is mechanical); the 8 voting/identity
   items are read *only* in `tests/voting_power.rs`, `tests/epoch_logs.rs`,
   `tests/uids.rs` (all three already feature-gated).
2. **Strategy codification (P-006 Batch 2 Groups 3 + 4 + 5 = 25 items)**:
   the coldkey-swap, root-claim, and admin/migration groups have live
   cross-pallet readers (`pallet-admin-utils` dispatch + tests + mock config;
   `pallet-proxy` auto-generated weight tables). A single P0 worker cannot
   safely remove them, but a *strategy ADR* can record the per-group risk
   profile, the cross-pallet consumers, and the recommended removal order so
   a future re-plan's first claim lands on a recipe the planner already
   audited.
3. **Public-testnet bootnode contract-drift guard**: the W-01
   `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` `bootnodes` field carries `Placeholder` libp2p
   multiaddrs (`/dns4/bootnode-1.myosu-testnet.example.com/tcp/30333/p2p/12D3KooWPlaceholderBootnode1PubkeyReplaceMe`),
   while the operator-deployable `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` already emits real
   `peer_id`s (the script calls `myosu-chain key generate-node-key` then
   `key inspect-node-key` and renders the libp2p multiaddr). The day an
   operator runs `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` and pastes the real `peer_id` into
   the manifest, nothing in CI catches a multiaddr shape that the chain
   binary's `--bootnodes` parser rejects. A contract-drift guard that
   round-trips a real `peer_id` through the manifest's `bootnodes` field
   shape closes that gap.
4. **PROMOTE-001 synthetic-NLHE unblock**: the only open `[!]` row
   (`PROMOTE-001` Promote nlhe-heads-up to promotable_local) has been parked
   on external artifact supply for ~3 months. F-007 and NEM-001B already
   proved the right pattern (mix-ladder substitute for poker quality; an
   independent reference benchmark that demonstrates the contract *shape*
   without needing the external artifact). The same pattern applied to
   PROMOTE-001: a synthetic zero-iteration NLHE checkpoint that round-trips
   through `verify_policy_bundle` so the W-02 bundle URL contract, the
   PROMOTE-001 promotion tier, and the `CanonicalPolicyBundle` builder are
   all proven against an artifact the repo owns. The unblock is *not* the
   PROMOTE-001 promotion (the external artifact is still required for the
   real positive-iteration exploitability ladder), it is the *contract* —
   the day external supply lands, the contract is already proven and the
   promotion slice is a one-line add.

- [ ] **[P1] `CHAIN-SDK-005` Author `docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md`: a strategy ADR that codifies the sequenced removal plan for the 25 remaining P-006 Batch 2 items (coldkey-swap 5, root-claim 11, admin/migration 9) the just-rowed CHAIN-SDK-003/004 work deliberately deferred**

  Spec: `gen-20260405-145446/p-006-stage0-surface-audit.md` `## Storage Census` `### Deferred storage (50)` (the 4 groups not yet removed: delegation/childkey/autostake 14, coldkey-swap 5, root-claim 11, admin/migration 9) + `docs/adr/000-template.md` (the established ADR shape `Status / Date / Deciders / Consulted / Informed / Related / Context / Decision / Consequences / Follow-up`).
  Why now: The p-006 audit identified 5 deferred-storage domains but did not sequence them. CHAIN-SDK-003 + CHAIN-SDK-004 ship Groups 1 + 2 (the two lowest-risk groups, both storage-isolated to feature-gated test modules) and leave Groups 3 + 4 + 5 (coldkey-swap, root-claim, admin/migration — 25 items total) untouched because each has live cross-pallet readers in `pallet-admin-utils` (dispatch + tests + mock config) and `pallet-proxy` (auto-generated weight tables). A future re-plan's first claim cannot safely remove a 5-item coldkey-swap group with 3 cross-pallet readers without the planner re-doing the cross-pallet reader audit. CHAIN-SDK-005 is the *single-decision-grade* slice that records the per-group risk profile + cross-pallet consumer map + recommended removal order in `docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md`, so the next re-plan's first claim lands on a recipe the planner already audited. No code change; pure planning-grade decision artifact, in the same shape the 15 existing ADRs (most recently `docs/adr/015-mccfr-review-gate.md` from NEM-007) follow.
