  Integration touchpoints: `docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md` (new file; the single artifact an operator / planner / future worker reads); `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (this row + the CHAIN-SDK-003 + CHAIN-SDK-004 rows gain a one-line cross-reference footer pointing at the ADR; the audit file `gen-20260405-145446/p-006-stage0-surface-audit.md` gets a one-line note at the top linking the ADR as the strategy that supersedes the audit's "Batch 2 not sequenced" caveat). No code change. No test change. No CI change. No crate change.
  Scope boundary: ADR-only. Do NOT modify any storage declaration, any test module, any dispatch, any mock config, any weight table, any CI job, any crate, any `construct_runtime!` block, any chain spec, any RPC, any gameplay surface, any solver, any policy-bundle builder, any miner / validator, any operator guide, any e2e harness, any zizmor policy, any audit file beyond the one-line cross-reference at the top, any other ADR, any INVARIANTS row, any SECURITY row, any CHANGELOG row. Do NOT touch the F-005 / F-006 Bitino items (sibling repo, out of scope). Do NOT touch the `pallet-subtensor` parallel directory (Plan 002's "remove duplicate ~150K lines" recommendation lives in a separate ADR the CEO roadmap does not own).

(2) The `## Decision` section names per-group: cross-pallet consumer map, recommended removal slice count, recommended slice order, and the recommended `construct_runtime!` / `pallet-admin-utils` / `pallet-proxy` follow-up for each group. (3) The `## Context` section cross-references the p-006 audit, the CHAIN-SDK-002 / 003 / 004 rows, and the `legacy-subtensor-tests` feature gate. (4) The `## Follow-up` section names 4 concrete follow-up items with a 1-line "claim at next refill tick" prompt for each. (5) `git log -1 --oneline` on `trunk` shows a single new commit titled `docs(adr): CHAIN-SDK-005 p-006 batch-2-recipe strategy ADR` + body referencing this row. (6) `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` carries the 3-row cross-reference footer at the end of CHAIN-SDK-003/004/005. (7) `gen-20260405-145446/p-006-stage0-surface-audit.md` carries the one-line note at the top linking the ADR. (8) `markdownlint docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md` (if available) or a hand-equivalent (`grep -c '^## ' docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md` returns ≥ 8) confirms the section shape.

- [ ] **[P0] `LIBP2P-001` Public-testnet bootnode contract-drift guard: a `tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` proof harness that fail-closes if the W-01 `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` `bootnodes` field shape stops being structurally replaceable byte-by-byte with the real libp2p multiaddr format `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` actually emits**

  Spec: `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` `## Field contract` `bootnodes` field + `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` (the operator-deployable script that already generates a real `peer_id` via `myosu-chain key generate-node-key --file <path>` and `key inspect-node-key --file <path>`, then renders `/ip4/<host>/tcp/<port>/p2p/<peer_id>`).
  Why now: The W-01 public-testnet contract (`ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`) was published with `Placeholder` libp2p multiaddrs in the `bootnodes` field (`/dns4/bootnode-1.myosu-testnet.example.com/tcp/30333/p2p/12D3KooWPlaceholderBootnode1PubkeyReplaceMe`). The operator-deployable `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` already emits the *real* shape the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser accepts: an `/ip4/<host>/tcp/<port>/p2p/<peer_id>` multiaddr where `peer_id` is the lowercase `12D3KooW...` base58-encoded ed25519 pubkey the `key generate-node-key` / `inspect-node-key` pair produces. The day an operator runs `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh --dry-run` and pastes the real `bootnode_multiaddr=` value from the script's stdout into the manifest's `bootnodes[0]`, nothing in CI catches a multiaddr shape that the chain's `--bootnodes` parser rejects. The `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` harness asserts the *field is present* (it does, today), but it does not assert the *value shape* is compatible with what `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` produces. A single `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` proof closes that gap.
  Codebase evidence: `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` lines 80-82 (the 2 placeholder `bootnodes` entries — `/dns4/bootnode-1.myosu-testnet.example.com/tcp/30333/p2p/12D3KooWPlaceholderBootnode1PubkeyReplaceMe` + `/dns4/bootnode-2.myosu-testnet.example.com/tcp/30333/p2p/12D3KooWPlaceholderBootnode2PubkeyReplaceMe`); `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` lines 78-89 (the `public_transport_addr` helper that renders `/ip4/<host>/tcp/<port>` for IPv4, `/ip6/...` for IPv6, `/dns/...` for DNS, matching the W-01 manifest's `/dns4/...` shape), lines 145-148 (the `node_cmd` `--listen-addr` + `--public-addr` pair that takes a multiaddr), and lines 152-153 (`peer_id="$(node_bin key inspect-node-key --file $node_key_file)"` + `bootnode_multiaddr="${public_addr}/p2p/${peer_id}"` — the literal `p2p/<peer_id>` suffix the chain binary parses). The existing `tests/e2e/two_node_sync.sh` already composes a 2-process libp2p topology on a single host and proves peer-discovery works (the harness's `bootnode_multiaddr` variable is exactly the `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` output shape), but no harness currently asserts the *manifest schema* is compatible with that shape. `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` exists but only asserts the manifest's *field* is present + the doc references the manifest, not the multiaddr shape.
  Owns: (a) New `tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` (~120 LOC, executable, fail-closed; mirrors the `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` shape). The harness (i) reads `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`, (ii) `yq` (or `grep` + `sed` if `yq` is not installed — the harness tolerates both with a documented fallback) extracts every `bootnodes[*]` entry, (iii) for each entry, asserts the multiaddr shape is one of three accepted forms: `/ip4/<ipv4>/tcp/<port>/p2p/<peer_id>`, `/ip6/<ipv6>/tcp/<port>/p2p/<peer_id>`, `/dns4/<hostname>/tcp/<port>/p2p/<peer_id>` (the three shapes `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` `public_transport_addr` helper emits), (iv) asserts the `<peer_id>` suffix is a 49-character base58 string starting with `12D3Koo` or `Qm` (the two `libp2p::PeerId` base58 encodings the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser accepts), (v) generates a *fresh* `peer_id` via `target/debug/myosu-chain key generate-node-key --file /tmp/bootnode-key-<UTC> && target/debug/myosu-chain key inspect-node-key --file /tmp/bootnode-key-<UTC>` (the exact same `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` invocation), (vi) computes `fresh_multiaddr="/ip4/127.0.0.1/tcp/30333/p2p/${fresh_peer_id}"`, (vii) round-trips the `fresh_multiaddr` through the manifest's `bootnodes` field shape (writes a copy of the manifest with the placeholder replaced by `fresh_multiaddr`, then `yq` / `grep` re-extracts the entry and asserts it equals `fresh_multiaddr` byte-for-byte), (viii) asserts the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser accepts the `fresh_multiaddr` (via `target/debug/myosu-chain --bootnodes "${fresh_multiaddr}" --help` — the chain binary's CLI parser should print the help banner without an `error: Invalid multiaddr` message; the exit code is 0 or 1 depending on the rest of the CLI surface, but the *stderr* is what we grep for `Invalid multiaddr`). The harness has 8 sub-checks, all fail-closed. (b) `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` gains a one-line comment at the top of the `bootnodes:` field referencing the new harness as the executable contract-drift guard (mirrors the existing top-of-file comment that references `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh`). (c) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a new `public-testnet-bootnode-roundtrip` job in the `active-crates` group (mirrors the `public-testnet-manifest` job shape: `needs: repo-shape`, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD: "1"`, the same `actions/checkout` SHA, the same `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` SHA, the same `Swatinem/rust-cache` v2 SHA, the same `cache-on-failure: true`) that runs `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` as its single step. The new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line is wired into `.github/zizmor.yml` with a `# reason:` justification matching the W-01..W-07 + CHAIN-SDK-002 discipline (the dtolnay count rises by 1 and the zizmor-policy harness's `rule_lines == justification_lines` 1:1 check stays green). (d) `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (the existing W-01 harness) gains a regression-guard sub-check that asserts the manifest's top-of-file comment references the new harness by name (so a future refactor that drops the cross-reference is loud). (e) `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` gains a one-line note in the `## Field contract` `bootnodes` field bullet pointing at the new harness as the executable contract-drift guard, mirroring the W-01 existing references to `public_testnet_manifest.sh`.
  Integration touchpoints: `tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` (new, 8 sub-checks), `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (1-line top-of-field comment), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (1 new CI job, 1 new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line), `.github/zizmor.yml` (1 new per-line ignore with `# reason:`), `tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (1 new regression-guard sub-check), `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (1-line `## Field contract` note). No crate change. No runtime change. No chain-spec change. No pallet change. No gameplay surface. No solver. No policy-bundle builder. No miner / validator.
  Scope boundary: Read-only contract-drift guard. Do NOT change the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser. Do NOT change `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh`. Do NOT replace the manifest's `Placeholder` multiaddrs with real `peer_id`s (that is the *operator's* job, deferred to the public-testnet deploy; the harness proves the manifest's *shape* accepts real `peer_id`s, not that the manifest *contains* real `peer_id`s). Do NOT add a host-network multi-host test (the W-04 multi-host determinism proof already covers multi-host composition; LIBP2P-001 is *schema* testing, not network testing). Do NOT add a CI job that requires a public network (the harness is `127.0.0.1` + a `target/debug/myosu-chain` subprocess; no live network). Do NOT change the W-01 public-testnet contract's other fields (`chain_spec`, `rpc_endpoints`, `subnet`, `promotable_games`, `authority_uris`, `healthcheck`, `last_rotated_at`). Do NOT change the `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` body. Do NOT change the `pallet-admin-utils` / `pallet-proxy` cross-pallet consumer map (that is CHAIN-SDK-005's scope). Do NOT touch the F-005 / F-006 Bitino items. Do NOT touch the W-02..W-07 surfaces.
  Acceptance criteria: (1) `tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` exists, is executable, has 8 fail-closed sub-checks, and exits 0 with all sub-checks green. (2) The 8 sub-checks: (i) `manifest.yaml` is parseable, (ii) `bootnodes` field is a YAML list with ≥ 1 entry, (iii) every entry matches one of the 3 accepted multiaddr shapes, (iv) the `<peer_id>` suffix matches the `^12D3Koo[A-Za-z0-9]{44}$` or `^Qm[A-Za-z0-9]{44}$` regex, (v) `target/debug/myosu-chain key generate-node-key` produces a fresh 32-byte ed25519 key + `key inspect-node-key` returns a valid `peer_id`, (vi) the `fresh_multiaddr` constructed from the fresh `peer_id` round-trips byte-for-byte through the manifest's `bootnodes` field shape (write-then-re-extract assertion), (vii) the chain binary's `--bootnodes "${fresh_multiaddr}"` CLI parser does not print `error: Invalid multiaddr` to stderr, (viii) the new `public-testnet-bootnode-roundtrip` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and runs the harness on every push / PR. (3) `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (the W-01 regression) still passes — its new sub-check confirms the new harness is referenced by name in the manifest's top-of-file comment. (4) `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` exits 0 with `rule_lines == justification_lines` (the new dtolnay rule + justification is 1:1). (5) `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` carries the one-line `## Field contract` `bootnodes` field bullet pointing at the new harness. (6) `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` carries the one-line top-of-field comment referencing the new harness.
  Verification: `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` (assert exit 0, all 8 sub-checks green); `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (assert exit 0, new regression-guard sub-check green); `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` (assert exit 0, `rule_lines == justification_lines`); `grep -q 'public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh' ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (assert 1 hit, the top-of-field comment); `grep -q 'public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh' docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (assert 1 hit, the `## Field contract` `bootnodes` field bullet); `grep -q 'public-testnet-bootnode-roundtrip' .github/workflows/ci.yml` (assert 1 hit, the new CI job).
  Required tests: (a) manifest YAML parseable, (b) `bootnodes` field shape matches one of 3 accepted multiaddr forms, (c) `<peer_id>` suffix is a 49-char base58 string starting with `12D3Koo` or `Qm`, (d) `myosu-chain key generate-node-key` + `inspect-node-key` round-trip produces a valid `peer_id`, (e) the fresh `peer_id` constructed multiaddr round-trips through the manifest schema byte-for-byte, (f) the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser accepts the fresh multiaddr, (g) the `zizmor_policy.sh` 1:1 line-justify check stays green, (h) the existing `public_testnet_manifest.sh` regression-guard sub-check stays green.
  Dependencies: None (the `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` script already produces the multiaddr shape, the W-01 manifest already declares the field, the `tests/e2e/two_node_sync.sh` harness already composes a libp2p topology, the W-01 public-testnet contract is the only new code surface the harness reads). A `target/debug/myosu-chain` binary must be buildable (the `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-chain` step the existing `public-testnet-manifest` CI job runs is sufficient).
  Estimated scope: S (1 e2e harness with 8 sub-checks + 1 manifest comment line + 1 CI job + 1 zizmor rule + 1 regression sub-check in the W-01 harness + 1 doc note; mirrors the W-01 / W-02 / W-03 / W-04 / W-05 / W-06 / W-07 established surface-shape).
  Completion signal: `tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` exists, the new `public-testnet-bootnode-roundtrip` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and runs the harness on every push / PR, the existing `public_testnet_manifest.sh` (W-01) harness still passes with the new regression-guard sub-check green, the `zizmor_policy.sh` 1:1 line-justify check stays green, the manifest carries the one-line top-of-field comment, and the operator-deployable doc references the new harness. **`lens:` CEO (closes the day-an-operator-deploys-testnet-finds-multiaddr-shape-rejected gap — the public testnet contract is now *machine-verifiably* ready to accept a real `peer_id` the operator pastes in from `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` stdout) + Eng (1 e2e harness + 1 CI job + 1 zizmor rule; the same surface-shape the W-01..W-07 + CHAIN-SDK-002 + CHAIN-SDK-003/004 waves established) + Design (the harness is the *operator-facing* proof that the public testnet contract has a live contract-drift guard, not a published contract that silently breaks when the operator pastes in a real `peer_id`).**

- [ ] **[P1] `PROMOTE-001-1B` Synthetic-NLHE policy-bundle example + e2e harness: prove the PROMOTE-001 `CanonicalPolicyBundle` + `verify_policy_bundle` contract round-trips against a synthetic zero-iteration NLHE checkpoint the repo owns (unblocking the `PROMOTE-001` `[!]` row's 4-month-parked contract shape without requiring the external NLHE artifact supply)**

  Spec: `genesis/plans/001-master-plan.md` `## Purpose / Big Picture` (the policy-bundle contract) + `genesis/plans/005-nlhe-promotion.md` (the PROMOTE-001 promotion tier) + `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` (the `CanonicalPolicyBundle` / `CanonicalPolicyDistributionEntry` / `CanonicalPolicyBenchmarkSummary` / `verify_policy_bundle` types) + `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` (the existing NLHE-specific `nlhe_policy_bundle` builder the W-02 / NEM-005B work established) + `genesis/plans/004-nlhe-benchmark-unblock.md` (the unblock-the-NLHE-promotion context the synthetic substitute is the contract-shape analog of).
  Why now: The `PROMOTE-001` `[!]` row has been parked on external NLHE artifact supply for ~3 months (the positive-iteration MCCFR training upstream is `isomorphism not found` per the NEM-001B row's CHANGELOG entry). The right unblock is *not* the positive-iteration exploitability ladder (that requires real external artifact supply) — it is the *contract shape*: the `CanonicalPolicyBundle` builder, the `verify_policy_bundle` round-trip, the W-02 `ops/bundles/liars-dice/` triple, and the `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/` URL contract all need to be proven against an artifact the repo owns. F-007 and NEM-001B already proved the right pattern (mix-ladder substitute for poker quality; an independent reference benchmark that demonstrates the contract *shape* without needing the external artifact). The same pattern applied to PROMOTE-001: a synthetic zero-iteration NLHE checkpoint + a synthetic policy bundle that constructs a `CanonicalPolicyBundle` with the `promotable_local` tier + a `tests/e2e/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` that proves `verify_policy_bundle` accepts the synthetic bundle and `bundle_hash` is byte-stable across 2 consecutive computations. The day external supply lands, the contract is proven and the promotion slice is a one-line add (replace the synthetic bundle with the real one). The unblock is the *contract*, not the *promotion*.
  Codebase evidence: `crates/myosu-games-poker/src/policy_bundle.rs` (the existing NLHE-specific builder; exports `nlhe_policy_bundle`, `NlhePolicyBundle` types; ~150 LOC); `crates/myosu-games-canonical/src/policy.rs` (the canonical types; exports `CanonicalPolicyBundle`, `CanonicalPolicyDistributionEntry`, `CanonicalPolicyBenchmarkSummary`, `PolicyPromotionTier::{Routed,Benchmarked,PromotableLocal,PromotableFunded}`, `verify_policy_bundle`, `sample_policy_action`, `compute_bundle_hash`); `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/examples/liars_dice_policy_bundle.rs` (the W-02 example that produces the on-disk Liar's Dice triple under `ops/bundles/liars-dice/liars-dice/bundle.json`); `crates/myosu-games-poker/examples/nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` (the NEM-005B example the W-02 wave added — already produces a real NLHE policy bundle, but depends on a real checkpoint file the worker has to point at; the synthetic substitute adds a `synthetic` mode that constructs a `NlheStrategyResponse` from a hand-built strategy map and round-trips it through the canonical types without needing a checkpoint file). The `tests/e2e/nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` (W-02 / NEM-005B proof) already asserts the on-disk triple is byte-stable + the `verify_policy_bundle` round-trip succeeds against a real checkpoint; the synthetic substitute adds a `synthetic` mode that asserts the same shape against a hand-built policy distribution.
  Owns: (a) New `crates/myosu-games-poker/examples/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` (~150 LOC; mirrors the `liars_dice_policy_bundle.rs` example shape). The example constructs a hand-built `NlheStrategyResponse` (a `Vec<(NlheEdge, f32)>` distribution over the 4 NLHE decision points: Fold, Call, Raise(2), Raise(3)) for 3 synthetic scenarios (a fold-equity scenario, a pot-odds scenario, a stack-depth scenario), passes each through `nlhe_policy_bundle(...)` to construct a `NlhePolicyBundle`, wraps each in a `CanonicalPolicyBundle { tier: PolicyPromotionTier::PromotableLocal, ... }`, and writes the triple to `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/synthetic/bundle.json` + `benchmark-summary.json` + `artifact-manifest.json` (the canonical on-disk triple the W-02 public-bundles doc established for Liar's Dice, mirrored for NLHE in synthetic mode). (b) New `crates/myosu-games-poker/tests/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` (4 unit tests): (i) `synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle_construction_does_not_panic` — the 3-scenario construction succeeds without panicking, (ii) `synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle_round_trips_through_verify` — `verify_policy_bundle(&bundle)` returns `Ok(())` (proves the contract shape is correct), (iii) `synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle_hash_is_byte_stable` — `compute_bundle_hash(&bundle)` returns the same 64-hex string for 2 consecutive calls (the W-02 byte-stability discipline), (iv) `synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle_distribution_sums_to_one` — the 3 `probability_ppm` distributions across each of the 3 scenarios sum to `1_000_000` (proves the canonical `probability_ppm: u32` discipline). (c) New `tests/e2e/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` (8 sub-checks; mirrors the W-02 `public_bundles_manifest.sh` + NEM-005B `nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` shape). The harness (i) runs `cargo run --example synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle`, (ii) asserts the on-disk triple under `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/synthetic/` exists with byte-stable `bundle_hash`, (iii) runs the example a second time and asserts the `bundle_hash` is byte-identical (the W-02 / NEM-001B byte-stability discipline), (iv) reads the on-disk triple and asserts every required field is present (per the W-02 public-bundles doc), (v) constructs an in-process `CanonicalPolicyBundle` from the on-disk JSON and asserts `verify_policy_bundle` returns `Ok(())`, (vi) asserts the `tier` field is `promotable_local` (the PROMOTE-001 promotion tier), (vii) asserts the on-disk `benchmark-summary.json` carries a `passing: true` field (the W-02 contract), (viii) asserts the `artifact-manifest.json` `provenance` field carries `synthetic: true` (so an operator can `diff` a real bundle against the synthetic one to confirm the contract shape before plugging in a real artifact). (d) `docs/operator-guide/public-bundles.md` gains a new `### Synthetic NLHE policy bundle` subsection that names the new bundle, the on-disk path, the `synthetic: true` provenance field, the `verify_policy_bundle` round-trip, the byte-stability guarantee, and a `curl`-style invocation example for an external operator who wants to confirm the contract shape before the real artifact supply lands. (e) `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/synthetic/` (the new on-disk triple) is committed to `trunk` and listed in `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/` as the synthetic sibling to the future real bundle (the directory layout mirrors the existing `ops/bundles/liars-dice/` shape). (f) `README.md` "Operator Path" gains a one-line pointer to the new doc subsection under the existing `### Public policy bundles` subsection, mirroring the W-02 discipline. (g) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a new `synthetic-nlhe-policy-bundle` job in the `active-crates` group (mirrors the W-02 / NEM-005B `nlhe-policy-bundle` job shape: `needs: active-crates`, `SKIP_WASM_BUILD: "1"`, the same `actions/checkout` SHA, the same `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` SHA, the same `Swatinem/rust-cache` v2 SHA, the same `cache-on-failure: true`) that runs `bash tests/e2e/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` as its single step. The new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line is wired into `.github/zizmor.yml` with a `# reason:` justification matching the W-01..W-07 + CHAIN-SDK-002 + CHAIN-SDK-003/004 + LIBP2P-001 discipline (the dtolnay count rises by 1 and the zizmor-policy harness's `rule_lines == justification_lines` 1:1 check stays green).
  Integration touchpoints: `crates/myosu-games-poker/examples/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` (new example), `crates/myosu-games-poker/tests/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` (new unit test module), `tests/e2e/synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.sh` (new e2e harness), `ops/bundles/nlhe-heads-up/synthetic/bundle.json` + `benchmark-summary.json` + `artifact-manifest.json` (3 new committed artifacts), `docs/operator-guide/public-bundles.md` (new `### Synthetic NLHE policy bundle` subsection), `README.md` (1-line pointer in `### Public policy bundles`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (1 new CI job, 1 new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line), `.github/zizmor.yml` (1 new per-line ignore with `# reason:`). No crate change outside the 3 listed. No runtime change. No chain-spec change. No pallet change. No gameplay surface. No miner / validator. No pallet-admin-utils / pallet-proxy.
  Scope boundary: Synthetic substitute only. Do NOT construct a real positive-iteration NLHE checkpoint (the upstream MCCFR training is `isomorphism not found`; that is a separate `genesis/plans/004-nlhe-benchmark-unblock.md` slice, not PROMOTE-001-1B). Do NOT touch the F-005 / F-006 Bitino items. Do NOT touch the W-01..W-07 surfaces.

(2) `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo test -p myosu-games-poker --quiet` exits 0 with the 4 new unit tests in `synthetic_nlhe_policy_bundle.rs` green. ## Next phase - RE-PLAN 2026-06-09 by designcritic, RE-PLAN task t_55e73cde

The 5 P0/P1 rows the 2026-06-08 re-plan (commit `018b374`) added are still
fresh and unclaimed on `trunk` — `CHAIN-SDK-003` (p-006 Batch 2 Group 1
PoW/faucet 8-item deletion), `CHAIN-SDK-004` (Batch 2 Group 2 voting/identity
8-item deletion), `CHAIN-SDK-005` (Batch 2 strategy ADR for the 25 remaining
items), `LIBP2P-001` (public-testnet bootnode contract-drift guard), and
`PROMOTE-001-1B` (synthetic NLHE policy-bundle example + harness). The
`[!]` row `PROMOTE-001` remains parked on external NLHE artifact supply;
PROMOTE-001-1B unblocks the *contract shape* but does not promote the
artifact. The 2 stale `[ ]` rows (`F-005`, `F-006`) are Bitino sibling-repo
items, deliberately out of scope.

What is NOT in the 5-row batch, in order of CEO leverage, is the
**public-testnet *integration* layer** the W-01..W-07 + LIBP2P-001 work
deliberately kept local:

1. **W-08 Public-testnet end-to-end live-read proof** — W-04 (multi-host
   determinism) + W-07 (live-read) are *local-process* proofs. W-08 wires
   them through the W-01 manifest's libp2p bootnode contract so the
   externally-verifiable read surface the CEO roadmap's "long" goal names
   is actually exercised against a public-shaped topology, not a
   `127.0.0.1:0` subprocess. The day an operator deploys the testnet, the
   `bundle_hash` + `miner_uid` + `emission` proof a public agent reads is
   the same proof CI gates on.
2. **OBS-001 Operator status dashboard** — operators and external agents
   have the W-01 manifest's `rpc_endpoints` to read, but no one-line
   status surface. A `myosu-testnet-status` CLI + a `testnet-status.md`
   doc + a CI-gated `healthcheck.json` liveness proof is the
   operator-experience gap. The `ops/kpi_registry.yaml` and
   `ops/scorecard.md` already exist as data; the CLI is the missing
   read-surface.
3. **CHAIN-SDK-006 First follow-on from CHAIN-SDK-005** — once the
   `docs/adr/016-p006-batch-2-recipe.md` strategy ADR lands, the next
   concrete slice the recipe sequences is the 5 coldkey-swap items (the
   smallest of the 3 remaining groups, with the simplest cross-pallet
   reader audit per the 2026-06-08 re-plan's CHAIN-SDK-005 evidence
   section). The recipe is the source-of-truth; CHAIN-SDK-006 is the
   first worker-claimable follow-on.
4. **PROMOTE-001-1C Testnet emission-from-advice proof** — W-07 proves
   *read* works against the live chain. The CEO roadmap's "emissions flow
   by measured quality" claim is a *write→wait→read* claim, and the
   existing harnesses (`emission_flow.sh`, `cross_node_emission.sh`,
   `epoch_per_uid_emission_sweep.sh`) prove the *static* shape. The
   *dynamic* feedback loop (submit advice → wait for tempo → observe
   `Emission(7)[uid]` change) is the missing testnet demo milestone.
5. **SEC-003 CI supply-chain hardening policy** — `SEC-001` (allowlist
   triage) and `SEC-002` (bincode decision) shipped. NEM-007 + INV-006
   covers the robopoker-fork review process. What is missing is the
   *CI-enforcement* side: `cargo --locked` in CI, no `[patch.crates-io]`
   of unknown provenance, no `git` URL deps in the default build. A
   documented policy + a proof harness is the productionization hook
   for the day a security reviewer asks "are you sure?" right before
   mainnet.

The 5 rows below are the concrete, individually-shippable next-phase items
the following execution tick claims. Each names the files it touches, states
how it is verified, and has a narrow scope boundary so a worker can claim
and finish it without re-discovering context. They are worker-ready, not
prose. The 2026-06-08 5-row batch is *not* re-stated here — it remains the
in-flight plan and the first execution tick should claim those rows first;
W-08 / OBS-001 / CHAIN-SDK-006 / PROMOTE-001-1C / SEC-003 are the *second*
tick's claim surface. Owner for the RE-PLAN row itself: designcritic,
2026-06-09.

- [ ] **[P0] `W-08` Public-testnet end-to-end live-read proof: a `tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` harness that wires the W-04 multi-host topology through the W-01 manifest's libp2p bootnode contract and proves a `myosu-play --read-solved --chain-endpoint <bootnode-discovered>` invocation returns `bundle_hash` + `miner_uid` + `emission` against the *public-shaped* topology, not a `127.0.0.1:0` subprocess**

  Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` `## Goals (sequenced now -> testnet)` `[long] Externally-verifiable read surface` (the CEO roadmap's "long" goal: "a human/agent fetches a solved result ... from the live testnet over a persistent endpoint, not a local subprocess smoke") + `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (the W-01 public contract) + `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (the operator-deployable doc) + `tests/e2e/two_node_sync.sh` + `tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` + `tests/e2e/live_read.sh` (the W-04 + W-07 local-process proofs W-08 elevates to a public-shaped topology).
  Why now: The W-04 multi-host validator determinism proof, the W-05 operator-bundle live proof, the W-07 dedicated-solver read surface, and the W-01 public-testnet manifest contract are all green. The LIBP2P-001 row in the 2026-06-08 5-row batch closes the *schema* gap (the manifest's `bootnodes` field shape). What remains is the *integration* gap: a single CI-gated proof that the W-01 manifest's `rpc_endpoints` + `bootnodes` + `chain_spec` fields, when consumed by the live chain binary, actually serve a `myosu-play --read-solved` request and return a non-trivial `bundle_hash`. W-08 is that proof. Without it, the day an operator deploys the testnet and an external agent pastes a real `peer_id` from `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh` into the manifest, the operator-facing claim is "CI says the schema is right" — but no CI proof says the *read* works against a public-shaped topology. CEO lens: the testnet's product value is the externally-verifiable read; W-08 is the test that proves the read is real, not a local-process smoke.
  Codebase evidence: `tests/e2e/two_node_sync.sh` composes a 2-process libp2p topology (1 bootnode + 1 peer) with real `127.0.0.1:<port>` addresses and `myosu-chain key generate-node-key` / `key inspect-node-key` peer_ids — exactly the shape W-08 needs, but with only 2 nodes; W-08 mirrors the shape with 5 nodes (1 bootnode + 1 miner + 2 validators + 1 orchestrator), matching the W-01 manifest's `authority_uris` count. `tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` proves the 5-node shape works for *determinism* (validators agree); W-08 reuses the 5-node composition and adds the *read* step. `tests/e2e/live_read.sh` (the W-07 proof) connects to a single-process subprocess, calls `discover_any_chain_visible_miner` + the `live_read` flow, and prints `status=solved bundle_hash=<64-hex>`; W-08 elevates this to a 5-node topology and asserts the `chain_endpoint` is `ws://<bootnode-discovered-host>:<port>` (not `ws://127.0.0.1:0`). The W-01 manifest's `bootnodes` field carries 2 placeholder `peer_id`s padded to the real ed25519 shape (the LIBP2P-001 contract-drift guard); W-08 generates *fresh* `peer_id`s at run time via the same `myosu-chain key generate-node-key` + `key inspect-node-key` invocation the LIBP2P-001 harness uses, so the proof exercises the real shape, not a placeholder. `crates/myosu-chain-client/src/lib.rs` exposes `discover_any_chain_visible_miner` + `evaluate_validator_agreement` + the `live_read` reporter — the existing read surface W-08 calls.
  Owns: (a) New `tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` (~200 LOC; mirrors the `tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` shape with a `live_read` step at the end). The harness (i) generates 5 fresh `peer_id`s via `target/debug/myosu-chain key generate-node-key --file /tmp/w08-key-<N>-<UTC> && target/debug/myosu-chain key inspect-node-key --file ...`, (ii) composes a 5-process topology: 1 bootnode (`--bootnodes` empty so it accepts all peers) + 1 miner (subnet 7, axon on a discovered port) + 2 validators (subnet 7, scoring on the same miner) + 1 orchestrator, (iii) writes a *generated* `target/e2e/w08/manifest-<UTC>.yaml` with the fresh `peer_id`s in the `bootnodes` field (the harness does NOT modify the committed `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`; the generated manifest lives in `target/e2e/w08/manifest-<UTC>.yaml` and is reaped on EXIT), (iv) waits for the bootnode's `chain_getHeader` to return parseable blocks (the LIBP2P-001 healthcheck pattern), (v) runs the W-07 `live_read` invocation against the *bootnode's* RPC URL (`ws://127.0.0.1:<bootnode-rpc-port>`), (vi) asserts `status=solved bundle_hash=<64-hex> miner_uid=<integer> emission=<integer>`, (vii) asserts the `bundle_hash` matches the SHA-256 the W-07 proof expects for the discovered miner's NLHE strategy (so the W-08 proof is the *same* read as the W-07 proof, just against a public-shaped topology), (viii) asserts the `emission` value at the bootnode endpoint equals the on-chain `Emission(7)[uid]` value the W-07 proof returns against the local subprocess (so the dynamic-feedback gap is closed: same emission, same miner, same advice, two different topologies). (b) New `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/public_testnet_live_read.rs` (~100 LOC; the example binary the harness invokes) that mirrors `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/live_read.rs` but takes `--manifest <path>` instead of `--chain-endpoint <url>`, parses the manifest's `bootnodes[*]` field to discover the bootnode's RPC URL, then runs the same `discover_any_chain_visible_miner` + `live_read` flow against the discovered URL. (c) New `## W-08 Public-testnet end-to-end live-read proof` section in `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (~30 lines) that names the harness, the example binary, the manifest-generation pattern, and the operator-facing interpretation of the `bundle_hash` + `miner_uid` + `emission` output. (d) New `public-testnet-e2e-live-read` CI job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the only step is `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh`). (e) New per-line zizmor rule in `.github/zizmor.yml` with `# reason:` justification (the established `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` 1:1 line-justify discipline). (f) New `## Public-testnet e2e live-read` subsection in `CHANGELOG.md` `## [Unreleased]` `### Added` that names the harness, the example, and the operator-facing "W-08 closes the public-shaped read gap" claim.
  Integration touchpoints: `tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` (new), `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/public_testnet_live_read.rs` (new), `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (1 new section), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (1 new CI job, 1 new `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` line), `.github/zizmor.yml` (1 new per-line ignore with `# reason:`), `CHANGELOG.md` (1 new subsection), `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (no change — W-08 generates its own `target/e2e/w08/manifest-<UTC>.yaml` rather than touching the committed manifest). No crate change. No runtime change. No chain-spec change. No pallet change. No gameplay surface. No solver. No policy-bundle builder. No miner / validator code change.
  Scope boundary: Read-only public-shaped integration proof. Do NOT change the W-01 `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (W-08 generates a *test-only* manifest under `target/e2e/w08/`; the committed manifest stays as-is for the LIBP2P-001 contract-drift guard). Do NOT replace the W-01 placeholder `peer_id`s with real `peer_id`s (operator's job, deferred to the public deploy). Do NOT change the chain binary's `--bootnodes` parser. Do NOT change `ops/deploy-bootnode.sh`. Do NOT add a host-network multi-host test (the W-04 multi-host proof already covers multi-host composition; W-08 is *public-shaped* integration, not network testing — the harness is `127.0.0.1` + 5 `target/debug/myosu-chain` subprocesses; no live network). Do NOT add a CI job that requires a public network. Do NOT change the W-01 public-testnet contract's other fields (`chain_spec`, `rpc_endpoints`, `subnet`, `promotable_games`, `authority_uris`, `healthcheck`, `last_rotated_at`). Do NOT change the `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` body. Do NOT touch the F-005 / F-006 Bitino items. Do NOT touch the F-013..F-019 portfolio-game promotion dossiers. Do NOT touch the in-flight LIBP2P-001 / CHAIN-SDK-003 / CHAIN-SDK-004 / CHAIN-SDK-005 / PROMOTE-001-1B rows (W-08 depends on LIBP2P-001 landing first so the `bootnodes` field schema is provably correct; the other 4 in-flight rows are independent).
  Acceptance criteria: (1) `tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` exists, is executable, has 8 fail-closed sub-checks, and exits 0 with all sub-checks green. (2) The 8 sub-checks: (i) `target/debug/myosu-chain key generate-node-key` produces 5 fresh 32-byte ed25519 keys, (ii) `key inspect-node-key` returns 5 valid `peer_id`s starting with `12D3Koo`, (iii) the 5-process topology composes (bootnode + miner + validator-1 + validator-2 + orchestrator), (iv) the bootnode's `chain_getHeader` returns parseable blocks at block 3, (v) the W-07 `live_read` invocation against the bootnode's RPC URL returns `status=solved bundle_hash=<64-hex>`, (vi) the `bundle_hash` matches the SHA-256 the W-07 proof returns against the local subprocess for the same miner's NLHE strategy, (vii) the `emission` value at the bootnode endpoint matches the W-07 proof's `emission` value (proves the public-shaped read returns the same dynamic state as the local read), (viii) the new `public-testnet-e2e-live-read` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and runs the harness on every push / PR. (3) `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (the W-01 regression) still passes — the W-08 harness does NOT touch the committed `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`. (4) `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` (the LIBP2P-001 regression) still passes — W-08's manifest-generation pattern reuses the LIBP2P-001 `key generate-node-key` + `key inspect-node-key` invocation. (5) `bash tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` (the W-04 regression) still passes — W-08's 5-process composition mirrors the W-04 composition. (6) `bash tests/e2e/live_read.sh` (the W-07 regression) still passes — W-08's read step is the W-07 read step elevated to a public-shaped topology. (7) `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` exits 0 with `rule_lines == justification_lines` (the new dtolnay rule + justification is 1:1). (8) `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` carries the new `## W-08 Public-testnet end-to-end live-read proof` section that names the harness, the example binary, the manifest-generation pattern, and the operator-facing interpretation of the output. (9) `CHANGELOG.md` `## [Unreleased]` `### Added` carries the new `## Public-testnet e2e live-read` subsection.
  Verification: `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` (assert exit 0, all 8 sub-checks green); `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_manifest.sh` (assert exit 0, W-01 regression); `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_bootnode_roundtrip.sh` (assert exit 0, LIBP2P-001 regression — the harness will not exist on disk until LIBP2P-001 lands; this acceptance criterion is a forward-looking regression guard); `bash tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` (assert exit 0, W-04 regression); `bash tests/e2e/live_read.sh` (assert exit 0, W-07 regression); `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` (assert exit 0, `rule_lines == justification_lines`); `grep -q 'public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh' docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (assert 1 hit, the new section); `grep -q 'public-testnet-e2e-live-read' .github/workflows/ci.yml` (assert 1 hit, the new CI job); `grep -q 'Public-testnet e2e live-read' CHANGELOG.md` (assert 1 hit, the new subsection).
  Required tests: (a) `key generate-node-key` + `inspect-node-key` round-trip produces 5 valid `peer_id`s, (b) the 5-process libp2p topology composes with real `127.0.0.1:<port>` addresses, (c) the bootnode's `chain_getHeader` returns parseable blocks at block 3, (d) the W-07 `live_read` invocation against the bootnode's RPC URL returns `status=solved`, (e) the `bundle_hash` is byte-stable across 2 consecutive W-08 runs (proves the read is deterministic against a public-shaped topology), (f) the `emission` value matches the W-07 proof's `emission` value (proves the dynamic state is the same), (g) the new CI job is wired, (h) the W-01 / LIBP2P-001 / W-04 / W-07 regression harnesses all stay green.

A `target/debug/myosu-chain` binary must be buildable (the `SKIP_WASM_BUILD=1 cargo build -p myosu-chain` step the existing `public-testnet-manifest` CI job runs is sufficient).

Coherent in one PR.
  Completion signal: `tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` exists and is green, `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/public_testnet_live_read.rs` exists and is reachable via `cargo run -p myosu-chain-client --example public_testnet_live_read -- --manifest target/e2e/w08/manifest-<UTC>.yaml`, the new `public-testnet-e2e-live-read` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and runs the harness on every push / PR, the W-01 / W-04 / W-07 / LIBP2P-001 regression harnesses all stay green, `bash tests/e2e/zizmor_policy.sh` exits 0 with the new dtolnay rule + justification 1:1, `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` carries the new section, and `CHANGELOG.md` carries the new subsection. **`lens:` CEO (closes the public-shaped read gap the CEO roadmap's "long" goal names; the day an operator deploys the testnet, the externally-verifiable read is CI-proven, not a local-process smoke) + Eng (1 e2e harness + 1 example binary + 1 doc + 1 CI job + 1 zizmor rule + 1 CHANGELOG; the W-01 / W-04 / W-07 / LIBP2P-001 surface-shape is the proven template) + Design (the operator-facing doc section names the manifest-generation pattern so a future operator can `bash tests/e2e/public_testnet_e2e_live_read.sh` against their own generated manifest and see the same `bundle_hash` + `miner_uid` + `emission` triple their public agent reads — the public testnet is *machine-verifiably* ready, not just *published*).**

- [ ] **[P1] `OBS-001` Operator status dashboard: a `myosu-testnet-status` example binary + a `tests/e2e/testnet_status.sh` harness + a `docs/operator-guide/testnet-status.md` doc that emit a one-line `TESTNET_STATUS endpoint=... peers=N finalized_block=H emission_per_tempo=X validator_set_size=Y` status from the W-01 manifest's `rpc_endpoints` + a CI-gated `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` liveness proof**

  Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` `## Lens verdicts` `Design` (the design lens's "A credible public testnet demo must let a human/agent ... SEE its provenance" claim — the testnet-status surface is the *operator-facing* sibling of the W-07 read surface) + `ops/kpi_registry.yaml` (the existing KPI registry; the testnet-status surface reads from it) + `ops/scorecard.md` (the existing scorecard; the testnet-status surface is the live version of the static scorecard) + `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (the W-01 contract; testnet-status reads the `rpc_endpoints` field) + `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` (the existing healthcheck example; testnet-status consumes its shape).
  Why now: The W-01 public-testnet manifest is published and the `rpc_endpoints` field is documented. Operators and external agents have a `wss://myosu-testnet.example.com:9944` URL to read. What they do NOT have is a one-line status surface that tells them, at a glance, whether the testnet is alive (`peers > 0`, `isSyncing = false`, `finalized_block > 0`, `emission_per_tempo > 0`, `validator_set_size > 0`). Today an operator has to `curl wss://...` and parse the JSON-RPC response by hand. The `ops/kpi_registry.yaml` already declares the 5 KPIs a status surface needs (`peers`, `isSyncing`, `finalized_block`, `emission_per_tempo`, `validator_set_size` — the first 3 are the existing `healthcheck.json` shape; the last 2 are the chain-side reads the existing `myosu-chain-client` examples already know how to do). What is missing is the *consumer* of the 5 KPIs. OBS-001 builds that consumer, in the same shape the W-02 `public_bundles_manifest.sh` + W-04 `multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` + W-07 `live_read.sh` established: 1 example binary + 1 e2e harness + 1 doc + 1 CI job + 1 zizmor rule. CEO lens: the operator-experience gap is the difference between "the testnet is published" and "the testnet is *operable*." OBS-001 is the operator-experience proof.
  Codebase evidence: `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` is the existing example `system_health` response body (peers=4, isSyncing=false, shouldHavePeers=true) — the testnet-status surface extends this shape with 3 chain-side reads. `crates/myosu-chain-client/src/lib.rs` exposes `ChainClient::get_block_height`, `ChainClient::get_finalized_block_hash`, `ChainClient::read_storage_value` (for `Emission(7)[uid]` reads), and the validator-set reader the W-04 multi-host proof uses — the 5 KPI reads OBS-001 needs. `ops/kpi_registry.yaml` `## guardrails` declares 5 guardrails the OBS-001 surface reads; the existing scorecard `ops/scorecard.md` is the static human-readable version; OBS-001 is the live machine-readable version. `tests/e2e/live_read.sh` + `tests/e2e/multi_host_validator_determinism.sh` are the W-07 + W-04 read-surface patterns OBS-001 reuses. `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` is the existing observability doc; OBS-001 is the testnet-specific slice of that doc.
  Owns: (a) New `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/testnet_status.rs` (~150 LOC; the example binary the harness invokes) that reads `--manifest <path>` (default: `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`), parses the `rpc_endpoints[*]` field, connects to each endpoint via `ChainClient::connect`, reads the 5 KPIs (`peers` + `isSyncing` from `system_health`, `finalized_block` from `chain_getHeader` + `chain_getBlock`, `emission_per_tempo` from `Emission(7)[uid]` storage read, `validator_set_size` from the validator-set reader), and prints a one-line `TESTNET_STATUS endpoint=<url> peers=<N> is_syncing=<bool> finalized_block=<H> emission_per_tempo=<X> validator_set_size=<Y>` output (one line per `rpc_endpoints[*]` entry; the harness asserts all lines parse and the W-01 manifest's primary `wss://myosu-testnet.example.com:9944` endpoint returns a non-error status). (b) New `tests/e2e/testnet_status.sh` (~120 LOC; 7 fail-closed sub-checks). The harness (i) composes a 1-node topology (the W-01 chain spec, 1 authority, no miner, no validator — a minimal live chain), (ii) writes a `target/e2e/obs001/manifest-<UTC>.yaml` with the live node's RPC URL in the `rpc_endpoints` field (the harness does NOT modify the committed `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`), (iii) runs `cargo run -p myosu-chain-client --example testnet_status -- --manifest target/e2e/obs001/manifest-<UTC>.yaml`, (iv) asserts the output line contains `endpoint=ws://127.0.0.1:<rpc-port>` + `peers=0` (single-node, no peers) + `is_syncing=false` + `finalized_block=1` (or higher) + `emission_per_tempo=0` (single authority, no validator scoring) + `validator_set_size=1`, (v) runs the example a second time and asserts the output is byte-stable (proves the 5-KPI read is deterministic), (vi) writes the same output to `ops/testnet/healthcheck.json` (the harness does NOT modify the committed `healthcheck.json`; the generated `healthcheck-<UTC>.json` lives in `target/e2e/obs001/` and is reaped on EXIT), (vii) asserts the new `testnet-status` CI job is wired in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. (c) New `docs/operator-guide/testnet-status.md` (~80 lines) that names the example binary, the 5 KPIs, the one-line output format, the operator-facing interpretation of each KPI (e.g., `peers=0` means "the testnet is up but not yet discovered the operator's node" — operator action: wait or check bootnode), and a `## CLI quickstart` section with the `cargo run -p myosu-chain-client --example testnet_status -- --manifest ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` invocation. (d) New `## Testnet status` subsection in `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` (~30 lines) that cross-references `testnet-status.md` and the 5-KPI surface as the live operator-facing slice of the observability doc. (e) New `testnet-status` CI job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the only step is `bash tests/e2e/testnet_status.sh`). (f) New per-line zizmor rule in `.github/zizmor.yml` with `# reason:` justification (the established `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` 1:1 line-justify discipline). (g) New `## Testnet status dashboard` subsection in `CHANGELOG.md` `## [Unreleased]` `### Added`.

- [ ] **[P1] `PROMOTE-001-1C` Testnet emission-from-advice proof: a `tests/e2e/testnet_emission_feedback.sh` harness + a `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/testnet_emission_delta.rs` example that submit advice → wait for a tempo → observe `Emission(7)[uid]` change → assert the change came from the validator's weight on the advice (not from genesis), closing the W-07 *read* / W-05 *operator-bundle* / `cross_node_emission.sh` *static* / `epoch_per_uid_emission_sweep.sh` *static* dynamic-feedback gap**

  Spec: `genesis/plans/000-ceo-testnet-roadmap.md` `## Goals (sequenced now -> testnet)` `[long] Externally-verifiable read surface` (the CEO roadmap's claim "emissions flow by measured quality" — the *dynamic* surface that the static-shape proofs do not cover) + `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/game-solver/src/run_epoch.rs` (the run-epoch loop that reads weights and writes `Emission(7)[uid]`) + `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` (the W-01 contract; PROMOTE-001-1C consumes the `subnet: 7` field) + `tests/e2e/cross_node_emission.sh` (the existing static-shape proof; PROMOTE-001-1C is the dynamic-feedback proof that *uses* the static shape).
  Why now: The W-04 multi-host proof, the W-05 operator-bundle live proof, the W-07 dedicated-solver read surface, the `cross_node_emission.sh` static emission proof, and the `epoch_per_uid_emission_sweep.sh` static per-UID emission proof are all green. What is missing is the *dynamic feedback loop*: a test that submits advice to a live chain, waits for a tempo, and observes `Emission(7)[uid]` change as a result. The CEO roadmap's "emissions flow by measured quality" claim is a *write→wait→read* claim, and the existing harnesses prove the *static* shape (emission exists at genesis, emission accumulates across a tempo, emission is divisible across UIDs) but do not prove the *dynamic* shape (emission *changes* when advice is submitted, and the change is attributable to the validator's weight on the advice, not to a genesis bootstrap or a stale-state artifact). PROMOTE-001-1C is that proof. CEO lens: the testnet's product value is "submit advice → get emission" — the dynamic feedback loop is what an external agent *actually* experiences when they integrate with the testnet.
  Codebase evidence: `tests/e2e/cross_node_emission.sh` composes a 5-process libp2p topology and asserts the *static* emission shape at genesis + after 1 tempo + after 2 tempos — the topology PROMOTE-001-1C reuses, with an added advice-submission step in the middle. `tests/e2e/epoch_per_uid_emission_sweep.sh` asserts the *static* per-UID emission accumulation across N tempos — the per-UID read pattern PROMOTE-001-1C reuses, with an added advice-submission step. `crates/myosu-chain-client/src/lib.rs` exposes `ChainClient::submit_advice` (the advice-submission RPC the W-05 operator-bundle live proof uses) and `ChainClient::read_storage_value` (the `Emission(7)[uid]` read the W-07 live-read proof uses) — the 2 RPC calls PROMOTE-001-1C threads together. `crates/myosu-chain/pallets/game-solver/src/run_epoch.rs` lines 200-300 (the run-epoch loop that reads `Weights(7)[validator_uid][miner_uid]` + `Incentive(7)[miner_uid]` and writes `Emission(7)[uid]`) — the on-chain dynamic-feedback surface PROMOTE-001-1C observes. `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml` `## Field contract` `subnet: 7` (the subnet the testnet serves; PROMOTE-001-1C targets this subnet).
  Owns: (a) New `tests/e2e/testnet_emission_feedback.sh` (~180 LOC; 8 fail-closed sub-checks; mirrors the `tests/e2e/cross_node_emission.sh` + `tests/e2e/epoch_per_uid_emission_sweep.sh` shape with an advice-submission step in the middle). The harness (i) composes a 5-process libp2p topology (1 bootnode + 1 miner + 2 validators + 1 orchestrator — the W-04 multi-host shape), (ii) waits for the bootnode's `chain_getHeader` to return parseable blocks at block 3, (iii) reads `Emission(7)[miner_uid]` at the *pre-advice* state and asserts the value is the genesis bootstrap value (`E_pre` = the value the `cross_node_emission.sh` static proof records at genesis), (iv) submits a non-trivial NLHE advice via `target/debug/myosu-chain-client submit-advice --chain-endpoint ws://127.0.0.1:<bootnode-rpc-port> --subnet 7 --miner-uid <uid> --advice <bundle-hash>` (the operator-bundle advice-submission path the W-05 live proof exercises), (v) waits for 1 tempo (the harness's tempo boundary is detected by reading the on-chain `Tempo(7)` storage value + waiting for the next `run_epoch` event in the validator logs), (vi) reads `Emission(7)[miner_uid]` at the *post-advice* state and asserts the value is `E_post > E_pre` (proves emission *changed* as a result of the advice), (vii) reads the on-chain `Weights(7)[validator-1][miner_uid]` + `Weights(7)[validator-2][miner_uid]` and asserts both validators submitted a non-zero weight for the advice (proves the emission change is *attributable* to the validator scoring, not to a stale-state artifact), (viii) asserts `E_post - E_pre` matches the sum of the 2 validators' weights × the tempo emission rate (proves the emission change is *exactly* the validator-weighted emission, not a rounding error or a per-UID truncation artifact). (b) New `crates/myosu-chain-client/examples/testnet_emission_delta.rs` (~100 LOC; the example binary the harness invokes) that mirrors the W-05 operator-bundle advice-submission shape but takes `--manifest <path>` (default: `ops/testnet/manifest.yaml`) and prints a one-line `EMISSION_DELTA subnet=7 miner_uid=<uid> e_pre=<integer> e_post=<integer> weight_v1=<integer> weight_v2=<integer> tempo_emission_rate=<integer>` output. (c) New unit test in `crates/myosu-chain/src/tests/emission_feedback.rs` (3 sub-tests) that asserts (i) the on-chain `run_epoch` loop reads the validator weights correctly, (ii) the `Emission(7)[uid]` write is the validator-weighted sum, (iii) the per-UID truncation policy (the NEM-003A / NEM-003B invariant) holds across a tempo boundary. (d) New `## PROMOTE-001-1C Testnet emission-from-advice proof` section in `docs/operator-guide/public-testnet.md` (~30 lines) that names the harness, the example binary, the operator-facing interpretation of the `e_pre` / `e_post` / `weight_v1` / `weight_v2` / `tempo_emission_rate` output, and the dynamic-feedback claim. (e) New `testnet-emission-feedback` CI job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the only step is `bash tests/e2e/testnet_emission_feedback.sh`). - [ ] **[P1] `SEC-003` CI supply-chain hardening policy: an ADR + a `tests/e2e/supply_chain_policy.sh` proof harness + a `.github/workflows/ci.yml` `supply-chain` CI job that enforce `cargo --locked`, no `[patch.*]` of unknown provenance, and no `git` URL deps in the default build, closing the productionization gap a security reviewer asks "are you sure?" about right before mainnet**

  Spec: `genesis/plans/008-security-debt-triage.md` (the SEC-001 / SEC-002 plan; SEC-003 is the *CI-enforcement* follow-on the plan did not address) + `INVARIANTS.md` INV-006 (the robopoker-fork review process; SEC-003 is the *CI-enforcement* sibling) + `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the existing CI config; SEC-003 adds a `supply-chain` job) + `docs/adr/013-zizmor-policy-decision.md` (the zizmor-policy ADR shape SEC-003 follows) + `docs/adr/012-bincode-1.3.3-decision.md` (the bincode-decision ADR shape SEC-003 follows) + `Cargo.toml` (the workspace root; SEC-003 enforces no `[patch.*]` sections) + `Cargo.lock` (the lockfile; SEC-003 enforces `cargo --locked` in CI).
  Why now: `SEC-001` (allowlist triage, 19 advisories classified) and `SEC-002` (bincode 1.3.3 decision accepted with hardened decode budgets) shipped. `NEM-007` + `INV-006` documented the robopoker-fork review process. What is missing is the *CI-enforcement* side: a documented policy that says "the default build must (a) compile against the committed `Cargo.lock` with `cargo --locked`, (b) carry no `[patch.crates-io]` or `[patch."https://..."]` of unknown provenance, (c) carry no `git` URL deps (`{ git = "https://..." }`) outside the 5 already-pinned `rev = "0471631..."` robopoker-fork pins." The 5 robopoker-fork pins in `crates/myosu-validator/Cargo.toml` + `crates/myosu-games-liars-dice/Cargo.toml` are the highest-risk attack surface (a fork commit can change the MCCFR algorithm) and NEM-007 / INV-006 covers the *review* process; SEC-003 is the *CI-enforcement* side that says "no NEW `git` URL deps or `[patch.*]` sections can land in the default build without an explicit ADR." A documented policy + a proof harness + a CI job is the productionization hook for the day a security reviewer asks "are you sure?" right before mainnet.
